**2026 test-cohort note (Chris, 2026-08-03 — delivery plan v1.4):** milestone order and gates are unchanged (M2 → M3 → M4 → M5, no calendar deadlines). A private test cohort of friends exercises each milestone as it lands — **auction (M3) and custom scoring are the headline feedback goals**. The custom scoring editor un-punt needs a spec changelog entry (Architect) before build; until then, templates-only (§7.3.3) remains LAW.

**Prerequisite reality:** this epic starts after repo Phases 0–4 + Live Mode land (delivery plan §3). Confirm that's true before M0 begins; if not, this build is blocked on the base app, not on itself.

## 3. Spec questions

*Anything where the spec is ambiguous, silent, or seems wrong. Write it here and STOP rather than improvising — per CLAUDE.md. Move to "Resolved" once Chris answers; the answer should also get folded back into the spec itself as a changelog entry.*

### Open

*(None open.)*

### Resolved

**Q11 — §7.2.1:190's notification recipient on the VOLUNTARY-LEAVE path: is the departing user notified, or only the commissioner? (filed by the M1 batch-15 remediation per R96, 2026-07-26; STOP-CONDITION HALT of R96's leave-path notification pin — the rest of the batch landed.)**

**Evidence.** §7.2.1's "Removal mechanics (all paths)" bullet at spec:190 reads: *"The removed user is notified with the outcome category (removed / left / franchise retired) and can be re-invited later — to any team, including their old franchise."* spec:192 then says *"Voluntary leave uses the same flow with `end_reason='left'`"*, which pulls the voluntary path INTO that bullet block. The parenthetical's three categories map to the §12.22 `end_reason` values that end a tenure (removed = `kicked`, left = `left`, franchise retired = `seat_retired`), and `left` is written by exactly ONE path in the repo: `leave_league` (063). Shipped behaviour: `remove_manager` notifies the REMOVED user on both takeover (`event: replaced`) and vacate (`event: removed`) — consistent with the bullet under either reading — while `leave_league` notifies **the sitting commissioner** ("A manager left <league>", `event: left`) and writes NOTHING to the departing user. The commissioner recipient is spec-silent: §7.2.1 never names the commissioner as a notification recipient anywhere (the nearest precedent is E54/spec:1734, "commissioner notified", for a near-simultaneous seat-claim collision — a different event, implemented in 062).

**Why this is a stop and not a Builder call.** Whether a user who just left gets a receipt is a PRODUCT decision (this file's §4 preamble routes product/scope calls to the spec changelog, not the decisions log), and both readings are defensible: **(a)** the block says "all paths", `left` can only mean the voluntary path, so the leaver IS a named recipient and the code omits a required notification; **(b)** "the REMOVED user" has no referent on a path where nobody was removed — the leaver performed the act synchronously and already has the RPC's success response, so the parenthetical is category VOCABULARY and the party who learns something new is the commissioner now holding an open seat. Under (a) the shipped code diverges from the spec; under (b) it is correct and the commissioner notification is a sanctioned addition. Pinning today's behaviour would silently cement (b) — precisely what R96's fix direction warned against.

**Options:**
- **(a) The leaver is notified too (recommended).** `leave_league` additionally calls the notifier for the departing user with the `left` category ("You left <league>"), keeping the commissioner notification as a spec-silent-but-useful addition recorded in the changelog. Correct under BOTH readings; ~6 lines plus one pin. The "it's noise" objection is real but bounded — §16's notification matrix (spec:1546) already defaults everything non-urgent to digests, and this is the least urgent class there is.
- **(b) Only the commissioner is notified (record the reading).** Rule that spec:190 addresses commissioner-initiated removals only; a one-clause §7.2.1 erratum says so and names the commissioner as the voluntary path's recipient. No code change — and the erratum is what stops this being re-found at every review.
- **(c) Both, with the leaver's as a digest-only receipt.** Needs a priority/digest flag `notifications` does not have, i.e. a new column for a row nothing consumes yet (F34: this notification has no consumer until L.A2.5/L.A2.7). Not recommended.

**Recommendation: (a)** — the only option correct under both readings, and smaller than the erratum (b) requires. Flagged as a product call because a notification's recipient set is very hard to change once a consumer exists.

**What's gated (precisely):** ONLY R96's leave-path notification pin, and any change to who `leave_league` notifies. Everything else in batch 15 landed on `feat/M1-L.A1.15-member-management`: R90–R95 and R97–R99 complete, full green proof chain, and R96's takeover/vacate row pins landed too (their recipient is the removed user under BOTH readings, so they cement nothing). **No task is blocked and no §5 blocker is filed** — the Q10 precedent for a question that gates a fix rather than a lane. L.A1.15 is done; the next lane heads (L.A2.x UI) do not consume this notification. Apply the ruling in whichever session next touches 063, or as a one-line PR; ledger row **F37** carries it.

**RULED 2026-07-26 (Chris): notify BOTH the user leaving AND the league manager (commissioner)** — recommendation (a), the reading correct under BOTH interpretations of §7.2.1:190. Applied same-day (M1 batch-16 remediation, same branch/PR #60): `leave_league` (063) now calls `notify_league_member_internal` for the departing user (`league_member` / "You left <league>" / `event: 'left'`) in addition to the pre-existing sitting-commissioner notification. **The deferred pin landed**: pgTAP 017 §S now pins BOTH the departing-user row and the commissioner row on §K's L1 leave (privileged 016-style read, `event='left'` scoped so it isolates from u5's earlier `removed` row). **Spec fidelity:** §7.2.1:190's "removed / left / franchise retired" categories already covered the departing user under reading (a) — now authoritative — but §7.2.1 named NO commissioner recipient anywhere, so the commissioner-too behaviour was spec-silent; folded in as spec erratum **v2.8.9** (§7.2.1:192 now names both recipients + changelog). **F37 flipped ✅**; the §5 Q11 note is cleared. Mechanics + the rest of batch 16 in D76.

**Q10 — `playoff_start_week` ↔ `regular_season_weeks` consistency: §7.3.1 promises a validation §7.3.8 never defines, and no formula fits the printed ranges (filed by the M1 batch-6 remediation per R62, 2026-07-22; substance observed by L.A1.6/D60(12), previously carried only as ledger row F25 — this entry puts it in the ruling queue).**

**Evidence:** §7.3.1's `playoff_start_week` row (D 15 · R 14–17 · note *"Validated against `regular_season_weeks`."*) promises a cross-field check, but §7.3.8's validation-bullet list contains no such bullet and prints no formula — and no formula is consistent with the printed ranges: strict continuity `playoff_start_week = regular_season_weeks + 1` is **unsatisfiable at `regular_season_weeks = 12`** (would need start 13; the printed floor is 14) and makes start 17 unreachable (would need a 16-week regular season; the ceiling is 15), while anything looser permits **gap weeks** (e.g. regular 12 + start 17 → weeks 13–16 belong to neither the season nor the playoffs, and nothing defines what an idle league week IS — L.D0's schedule engine has no such concept). With no check at all — the shipped state — an **overlap** is expressible: `regular_season_weeks = 15` + `playoff_start_week = 14` parses clean and `validateLeagueSettings` accepts it (L.A1.6 implemented exactly the task-enumerated §7.3.8 bullets, deliberately not improvising a formula — D60(12)). The §7.3.8 arithmetic bullet (`start + rounds × weeks_per_round − 1 ≤ 18`) bounds the playoff END only; it says nothing about the seam.

**Options:**
- **(a) Strict continuity + range erratum (recommended).** Rule `playoff_start_week = regular_season_weeks + 1`; spec erratum adjusts the printed R from 14–17 to **13–16** (13 reachable only at a 12-week season) and the schema range follows. No overlap, no gap weeks, no new concepts; the field becomes fully derived — the wizard can render it read-only next to `regular_season_weeks` (the `playoff_byes = 'auto'` precedent), with the stored value kept for §12.1 compat. Incumbent-consistent: ESPN/Yahoo/Sleeper all derive the playoff start; none lets a commissioner express an overlap.
- **(b) No-overlap lower bound only.** Rule `playoff_start_week ≥ regular_season_weeks + 1`; printed ranges stay. Kills the overlap but leaves gap weeks expressible, and gap-week semantics (standings? waivers? locks?) would then need defining somewhere before M4's schedule engine — a bigger spec debt than the seam check itself.
- **(c) Explicit won't-validate ruling.** Record that §7.3.1's note is aspirational in v1; the per-field ranges are the only guard and seam consistency is the commissioner's problem. Cheapest, but ships a settings surface that accepts playoffs starting before the season ends — and the note column then needs an erratum anyway (it currently promises otherwise).

**Recommendation: (a).** It is the only option with neither silent overlap nor undefined gap-week semantics; the cost is a two-line erratum (§7.3.1 R column + note, one §7.3.8 bullet) plus a ~6-line check in `validateLeagueSettings` with boundary pins — one small PR once ruled.

**What's gated (precisely):** NOT the current lane heads — engine is at L.A1.10 and schema at L.A1.11, neither touches playoff-week validation, so **no §5 blocker is filed**. The ruling gates **L.A1.12**: `create_league` (+ the L.A2.1 wizard on top of it) is the first surface that can PERSIST an inconsistent pair — creation must not ship on the unruled validator. L.A1.13's settings PATCH is the second exposure, not the first (the F25 row's original routing said L.A1.13 — corrected per R62). L.A1.6 itself remains complete; this question is bookkeeping mis-filed at first, not a new halt.

Implementation: seam check in `validateLeagueSettings` with a per-field message + the 13–16 schema range in `league-settings.ts`; the old live-probe overlap pair (regular 15 + start 14) is now the pinned REGRESSION TRAP (exact message, sole error), all four boundary edges (12→13/13→14/14→15 defaults/15→16) pinned passing, one-past pinned failing both directions, and the check applies even at `playoff_teams = 0` — the field is derived, not conditional (D67(1); the D60(5) points-only skip covers only the playoff-END arithmetic bullet). Defaults (14+15) and the round-trip fixture (13+14) already satisfied (a) — pinned, no value change needed. The wizard's read-only-derived rendering (the `playoff_byes = 'auto'` precedent, stored value kept for §12.1 compat) is L.A2.1's build concern — ledgered as **F27**. **Q9 — ESPN's verified in-product default kicking table (60+ FG tier) — and two D/ST micro-categories — cannot be expressed by the App B.4 catalog: ESPN template parity gap (filed by L.A1.9, 2026-07-21; STOP-CONDITION HALT per the task's re-verify rule — "Divergences from App B: stop and file a spec question").**

L.A1.9's build-time re-verification (task text item 1) ran the full sanctioned source pass on 2026-07-21 via the in-app browser (the L.A1.7 working path). **The F19 obligations themselves both RESOLVED cleanly** — evidence below, recorded here so no session refetches it — but the same pass surfaced a **new** App B divergence that the ESPN templates cannot express, and the task text is explicit that a divergence is a stop. No migration was written; nothing was seeded.

**What was retrieved (all 2026-07-21, in-app browser):**

1. **ESPN Scoring-Formats article** (support.espn.com/hc/en-us/articles/360003914032-Scoring-Formats) — re-fetched; standard PA table matches v2.8.3's transcription value-for-value (0/1–6/7–13/14–17/18–27/28–34/35–45/46+ at +5/+4/+3/+1/0/−1/−3/−5); the R50 finer custom-options PA list (2–6/18–21/22–27) still present, still values-free; nine YA buckets in custom options, **no standard YA values** (the F19(a) gap, reconfirmed). Standard offense: pass 0.04/TD 4/INT −2/2pt 2 · rush 0.1/6/2 · rec 0.1/6/2 (PPR +1/rec) · fumble lost −2 · KR/PR/fumble-recovery TD 6. **Standard kicking (the new finding): FG 0–39 = 3 · 40–49 = 4 · 50–59 = 5 · 60+ = 6 · PAT 1 · FG missed (any) = −1; no PAT-missed penalty.**
2. **ESPN D/ST article** (support.espn.com/hc/en-us/articles/115003847231) — re-fetched; split model reconfirmed ("D/ST begins each game with 10 points… given up 0 points (worth 5 points)… allowed 0 offensive yards (worth 5 points)") → the `def_ya_0_99 = +5` anchor. Also publishes two PAT micro-rules: **D/ST safety during a PAT = +1; D/ST returns the ball to its own end zone during a PAT = +2** (see finding 2 below).
3. **ESPN in-product defaults — the F19 pull (the product's own settings source, no auth):** `lm-api-reads.fantasy.espn.com/apis/v3/games/ffl/seasons/2026/segments/0/leaguedefaults/1?view=mSettings` (Standard) and `…/leaguedefaults/3?view=mSettings` (PPR). Full `scoringSettings.scoringItems` payloads, verbatim (statId, points, positional overrides — `{"16": v}` = applies to D/ST):
   - Standard (`leaguedefaults/1`): `[20,−2] [72,−2] [85,−1] [89,0,{16:5}] [90,0,{16:4}] [91,0,{16:3}] [92,0,{16:1}] [95,0,{16:2}] [96,0,{16:2}] [97,0,{16:2}] [98,0,{16:2}] [99,0,{16:1}] [123,0,{16:−1}] [124,0,{16:−3}] [125,0,{16:−5}] [128,0,{16:5}] [129,0,{16:3}] [130,0,{16:2}] [132,0,{16:−1}] [133,0,{16:−3}] [134,0,{16:−5}] [135,0,{16:−6}] [136,0,{16:−7}] [3,0.04] [24,0.1] [42,0.1] [86,1] [209,1,{16:1}] [19,2] [26,2] [44,2] [206,2,{16:2}] [80,3] [4,4] [77,4] [198,5] [25,6] [43,6] [63,6] [93,6,{16:6}] [101,6,{16:6}] [102,6,{16:6}] [103,6,{16:6}] [104,6,{16:6}] [201,6]`
   - PPR (`leaguedefaults/3`): identical **plus `[53,1]`** (receptions = 1) — nothing else differs.
   - Interpretation method (recorded for auditability — the payload is id-coded): the value multiset aligns one-for-one with the Scoring-Formats article's standard tables (offense 0.04/4/−2/2 · 0.1/6/2 · 0.1/6/2 · −2; PA +5/+4/+3/+1/−1/−3/−5 in the contiguous id runs 89–92 and 123–125; D/ST 2/2/2/2/1 + four 6-pt return TDs; kicking 3/4/5/6/1/−1), with **zero-valued buckets omitted from the payload** — PA 18–27 and YA 300–349 are exactly the article's 0-point rows and exactly the ids missing from the otherwise-contiguous runs. Two independent anchors pin the YA read: the head anchor `def_ya_0_99 = +5` (D/ST article) and the omitted-zero convention proven on the PA side.
   - **F19(a) RESOLVED — ESPN standard YA values (ids 128–136 in published-bucket order):** `<100 = +5 · 100–199 = +3 · 200–299 = +2 · 300–349 = 0 (omitted) · 350–399 = −1 · 400–449 = −3 · 450–499 = −5 · 500–549 = −6 · 550+ = −7`. Anchor matches. Unambiguous.
   - **F19(b) RESOLVED — the product's default model renders the STANDARD bucketization:** the default payload contains the standard list's ids (PA 1–6 present at +4; 18–27 absent-as-zero) and none of the finer custom-options buckets (no 2–6/18–21/22–27 items). The finer list is the LM custom bucket menu only; it is score-invisible at standard values as R50 recorded. The seeded def_pa keys stand; no additional keys needed; the templates (when they land) relate to the finer list as: not rendered by the default product, representable only in the v1.1 custom unlock (where F21's family-grouping applies).
4. **Yahoo SLN6489** (help.yahoo.com/kb/SLN6489.html) — full defaults retrieved (accordion DOM extraction): offense 0.04 (25 yds/pt) · pass TD 4 · **INT −1** · 0.1 rush/rec · TDs 6 · return TD 6 · off-fum-ret TD 6 · one combined 2-pt category = 2 · fumble lost −2 · **receptions 0.5 (the platform default = Half PPR, as §7.3.3 says)**; kicking FG 0–19/20–29/30–39 = 3 · 40–49 = 4 · 50+ = 5 · PAT 1 (**no miss penalties in defaults**; the sub-40 finer buckets are score-equal to `fg_0_39 = 3`); D/ST sack 1 · INT 2 · FR 2 · TD 6 · safety 2 · blocked kick 2 · KR/PR TD 6 · PA exactly the shared family: 0 = 10 · 1–6 = 7 · 7–13 = 4 · 14–20 = 1 · 21–27 = 0 · 28–34 = −1 · 35+ = −4 (single model, no YA — per spec). **Every Yahoo cell expressible in catalog keys; App B.1 Yahoo cells all reconfirmed.**
5. **Sleeper scoring-options article** (support.sleeper.com/en/articles/3998131-scoring-settings) — categories reconfirmed (PA = shared family buckets; YA buckets = ESPN's nine; FG buckets 0–19/20–29/30–39/40–49/50+, **no 60+ tier**) — but the article publishes **no default values**, and a support-center search ("default league settings") finds no Sleeper defaults article. Sleeper's in-product creation defaults sit behind account login (out of bounds). **Per the house verification rule (this task's charter): Sleeper's six template value-sets ship as App B best-known values FLAGGED unverified (ledger row on landing), never silently trusted.**

**The blocking finding (1): ESPN's default kicking table has a 60+ = 6 tier — App B.4's three-bucket shape (`fg_0_39`/`fg_40_49`/`fg_50_plus` at 3/4/5) cannot express it.** Product-confirmed twice, independently: the Scoring-Formats standard table (60+ = 6, 50–59 = 5) and both in-product default payloads (`[198,5]` = FG 50–59, `[201,6]` = FG 60+ — 198/201 are the only unaccounted 5/6-valued kicker ids, and their values match the article exactly). This is not a value drift on an existing key — it is a **category the engine's namespace cannot represent**: no `fg_50_59`/`fg_60_plus` keys exist, no columns exist, and — decisive — **the Sleeper feed has no 60+ signal** (`fgm_50p` aggregates everything ≥50; the L.A1.7 adapter audit lists sub-40 buckets but nothing above 50), so even new keys would have no data source on the current tier. Consequence: cent parity for ESPN templates is unachievable as-is on made 60+ FGs (order ~5–15 league-wide per NFL season; −1 pt each vs ESPN's product).

**The blocking finding (2), same class, smaller:** ESPN's defaults score two D/ST PAT micro-categories — PAT-safety = +1 (`[209,1,{16:1}]`) and PAT-return = +2 (`[206,2,{16:2}]`), both also published in the D/ST article. No catalog keys, no columns, no feed signal on any current provider tier (order ~1–3 plays league-wide per season).

**Why this is a stop and not an L.A1.7-style recorded erratum:** L.A1.7's charter explicitly licensed "seed what ESPN actually publishes and note the delta" (additive derived keys, no storage or feed dependency). L.A1.9's charter says the opposite — "Divergences from App B: stop and file a spec question (parity guarantee is the product promise — never silently ship drifted values)" — and this divergence has real options with product impact (below), including one that would add player_stats columns (schema beyond the task). Value-level per-platform differences (Yahoo's no-miss-penalty kicking, ESPN's no-PAT-miss) are NOT treated as divergences: B.1 defers K/D-ST to "each platform's own tables (⚠ verify)", so the verified platform table governs those cells; the stop is specifically for categories the catalog/namespace cannot express.

**Options:**
- **(a) Documented parity exception (recommended).** ESPN templates ship `fg_50_plus = 5`; the 60+ extra point and the two PAT micro-categories become a **named, documented parity exception** in §7.3.3/App B (spec changelog entry): "ESPN parity is cent-exact except made 60+ FGs (−1 pt vs ESPN) and D/ST PAT-safety/PAT-return (unscored) — categories the current data tier cannot observe." Rationale: the alternative keys would be **permanently pending/zero** for lack of any feed signal — a standing E61 state manufactured on every ESPN league every week is strictly worse than a documented ~1-pt/season-scale exception; and the parity guarantee's operative promise ("a migrating league's scores feel identical") survives a documented exception, not a standing honest-badge.
- **(b) Add `fg_50_59`/`fg_60_plus` (+ PAT-micro) keys and columns now.** Expresses the table, but no provider signal exists to fill them — every ESPN league shows permanent pending on those keys (E61), and it adds player_stats columns from inside a task chartered for `is_template` + seed only.
- **(c) = (a) + a September F-row:** the F10 raw-endpoint inspection window additionally checks whether Sleeper's raw feed carries a 60+ FG spelling (e.g. `fgm_60p`) and PAT-play detail; if a signal exists, upgrading ESPN kicking parity becomes the one-PR §23.5 data task it should be, and the exception shrinks or closes.
- **Recommendation: (a) + (c)'s September check.** One ruling line unblocks L.A1.9; the post-ruling build is mechanical (all six platforms' evidence is already in this entry).

**What's blocked:** L.A1.9 (migration 058 + pgTAP 012 + the 6 template rows) and the schema lane behind it (L.A1.11), plus L.A1.10/L.A1.12's template-row dependencies. **Not blocked:** L.A1.6 (engine lane, pure TS — still open and unstarted). F19 stays ⬜ Open (it discharges only when the ESPN rows land) but both its sub-obligations are answered above; the landing session should cite this entry in the 058 banner rather than refetching.

**RULED 2026-07-22 (Chris): option (a) + (c).** Ship `fg_50_plus = 5`; the ESPN 60+ FG tier (6 pts) and the two PAT micro-categories (+1 PAT-safety, +2 PAT-return) are **NAMED PARITY EXCEPTIONS**, not pending categories — recorded as a spec erratum (**v2.8.5**: §7.3.3 parity-guarantee erratum + App B.4 annotation + changelog) with the rationale (provider-unobservable, ~5–15 plays league-wide per season, permanent-pending would violate E61 semantics). **Condition:** the exceptions must be user-visible in the ESPN templates' description/detail metadata — a plain-language line a commissioner can read — not only in migration banners. **(c):** the September F10 check additionally looks for a Sleeper raw-feed 60+ spelling (e.g. `fgm_60p`) and PAT-play detail; if a signal exists, the tier becomes expressible as a one-PR §23.5 data task and the exception lifts — path noted in the erratum. Implementation: migration **058** + pgTAP **012** + `src/lib/leagues/scoring/templates.ts` (D59); the exception line lives in `scoring_systems.description` (the schema's existing detail column — a natural home, no schema change needed) and is pinned verbatim by pgTAP 012 and the templates tests. Sleeper's unpublished default values ship App-B-flagged per the house rule — ledger **F24**. B3 cleared; F19 flipped ✅.

**Q8 — client-writable league state: the spec-kept owner FOR ALL policy vs server-authoritative (§8.1) (filed by the M1 batch-2 review, R37/R38, 2026-07-20).** The review live-proved an ordinary authenticated user forging `scoring_rules_snapshot` + `status='drafting'` (and able to hard-DELETE a league, cascading `league_members`/`team_managers`) through 001's "League owners can manage" FOR ALL policy — which spec §12.1 explicitly said to keep — and a commissioner client-reseating `league_members.team_id` with no `team_managers` stint (cache⇄history divergence) through §12.2's printed commish FOR ALL policy.
**RULED 2026-07-20 (Chris): once created, league state is server-authoritative — no direct client DML.** Replace 001's "League owners can manage" FOR ALL with owner SELECT only (league creation already happens via RPC per the M1 task specs; the §15.1 deletion flow is a commish soft delete via RPC, so no client DELETE verb is retained — verified against the spec before implementing). Remove client UPDATE on `league_members` seating fields — reseating happens only via the L.A1.12–15 RPCs. The spec's "keep the owner policy" instruction is **superseded**: it predates 040's protected columns (`scoring_rules_snapshot`, `status`, `settings`), which turned a benign legacy policy into a forgery surface. Recorded in spec **v2.8.2**; implemented as migration **054** (D52).

**Q7 — Q4-application conflicts the conflict report didn't surface (filed 2026-07-20; STOP-CONDITION HALT of L.A1.1).** Recording the Q4 ruling (usernames 5–20, permanent) against the actual codebase hit two conflicts that C5 never surfaced, tripping the Builder session's ruling-vs-codebase stop condition — the migration was **not** written:

1. **AI persona accounts live in `profiles.username` with hyphenated handles** — `fieldscout-ai` (system owner) plus the ~19-persona roster (`bathew-merry-ai`, `zj-jachariason-ai`, …; `src/lib/personas/roster.ts`), created as real auth users + profiles rows by `scripts/seed-ai-personas.ts:88`, **existing in production** (the personas feature is live). *[Corrected — see D49(9)/R36: the roster personas are `ai_personas` rows, not profiles rows; only `fieldscout-ai` holds a profiles row. The conflict (and the ruling below) stands either way — the exempt pattern must cover every persona-shape handle a privileged writer seeds.]* A blocklist test *enforces* the `-ai` suffix (`blocklist.test.ts:41`), and the handles key avatar file paths + public URLs. Every one violates the `[a-z0-9_]` charset §7.2 gives the username contract — the CHECK as ruled would **fail the prod push** and break persona seeding. Needs your call: **(a)** exempt persona/system accounts — the CHECK becomes `human-pattern OR persona-pattern` (e.g. hyphenated, `-ai`-suffixed). Bonus: keeping hyphens out of the human charset means **no human can ever register a `*-ai` handle** — free namespace separation for the AI-transparency convention; **(b)** allow hyphens for everyone (loses that separation); **(c)** rename personas to underscores (touches public URLs, avatar files on disk, the blocklist tests, and any indexed content). **Recommendation: (a).**
2. **The account-settings page ships a live username-**change** flow** (`src/app/app/settings/page.tsx:173–181` — editable field, saves `username` with its own 3–30 validator), contradicting PERMANENT. Related definitional wrinkle: "no changing after creation" needs a boundary — signup *creates* the profile with an auto-generated `user_xxxxxxxx` placeholder, and the username-selection page (`/username`) is itself an UPDATE, so a literal no-update-after-creation guard would break onboarding. **Recommendation:** permanent = **after explicit selection** (the placeholder is pre-selection); remove the username field from settings (display-only `@handle`); typo/regret recovery is a support/admin path, not self-serve; a DB-level permanence guard is deferred until the selection flow moves server-side (an RPC that can tell selection from rename).
3. *(Minor, listed for completeness:)* `scripts/seed-dev-user.ts` seeds username `'dev'` (3 chars) — breaks under the 5-minimum; mechanical rename (e.g. `dev_user`/`dev_pro`) ships with the Q4 migration.

**What's blocked:** L.A1.1 (migration 040 carries the Q4 constraint per your ruling) and the serialized schema lane behind it. **Not blocked:** L.A1.6 (engine lane, pure TS). The Q5/Q6 rulings had no conflicts and are fully recorded/applied. One line from you on (1) and (2) unblocks the lane.
**RESOLVED 2026-07-20 (Chris): recommendations accepted for items 1, 2, and 3.** (1) Persona exemption **(a)**: the DB CHECK is `username ~ '^[a-z0-9_]{5,20}$' OR username ~ '^[a-z0-9]+(-[a-z0-9]+)*-ai$'` — human charset stays hyphen-free, so no human can register a `*-ai` handle (enforced at the DB layer by a 025-pattern guard trigger blocking persona-pattern usernames for `authenticated`/`anon`, since the CHECK alone can't tell a persona insert from a hostile PostgREST rename). (2) Permanent = **after explicit selection** — the signup-time `user_xxxxxxxx` placeholder is pre-selection and the `/username` page's UPDATE is the sanctioned selection write; the settings-page username field becomes **display-only**; typo/regret recovery is a support/admin path; a DB-level permanence guard is deferred until the selection flow moves server-side (documented residual: a determined user can still rename to a *valid human* name via direct PostgREST until then). (3) `seed-dev-user.ts` username `'dev'` → `'dev_user'`. Implementation: L.A1.1/040 (D49). B2 cleared.

**Q4 — username identity contract: §7.2 vs deployed reality vs spec-auth-profiles (filed per C5, 2026-07-20).** §7.2 requires `username` be **citext, 3–20 chars `[a-z0-9_]`**, and treats usernames as **changeable** ("league surfaces update live"; E52 tests a mid-season rename). Deployed reality: plain `TEXT UNIQUE NOT NULL`, zero DB constraints; the only validation is a client-side regex allowing **3–30 chars, must start with a letter** (lowercased app-side at write); and `spec-auth-profiles.md` says usernames are **permanent in V1**. Three product calls needed: (a) do existing 21–30-char handles stay valid (grandfather) or get migrated? (b) citext conversion vs a `lower(username)` unique index? (c) changeable (per §7.2) or permanent (per auth spec) for v1? **Recommendation:** grandfather existing handles; enforce §7.2's charset + 3–20 length on *new/changed* usernames only (DB CHECK scoped via trigger on change); take the `lower(username)` unique index instead of a citext type migration (same case-insensitive semantics, no column rewrite); adopt §7.2's *changeable* contract — league surfaces join on `user_id` so renames are already safe, and the auth spec's "permanent" reads as a V1 simplification this epic supersedes (fold into both specs' changelogs on resolution). **M1 interim (D36, not gated on this):** invite-by-username matches via `lower(username)`; no column migration ships until you rule.
**RULED 2026-07-20 (Chris): usernames are minimum 5 characters, maximum 20, and PERMANENT (no changing after creation).** Supersedes both §7.2's 3–20/changeable contract and the deployed 3–30 client rule. No production users → no grandfathering: the DB constraint itself moves to 5–20 as part of the schema work (Builder's call on home: **folded into 040** — noted in tasks-M1 L.A1.1). Uniqueness stays case-insensitive via a `lower(username)` unique index (no citext conversion). Recorded in spec **v2.8** (§7.2 table + prose; E52 obsoleted). **Application halted same-session on Q7** (persona `*-ai` handles + the settings-page rename flow — neither surfaced by the conflict report); the ruling itself stands.

**Q5 — transactional email vendor for seat-targeted invites (filed per C6, 2026-07-20).** §7.2 makes email the **primary** invite channel ("a real email is sent with a claim link") — and the repo has **zero** outbound-email capability (no email lib, no edge functions, production SMTP commented out; only Supabase Auth's built-in mails + the local dev capture inbox). Needed from you: vendor choice, API key, and a `fieldscout.gg` sender domain (DNS records). **Recommendation:** Resend (Next.js-native, template-friendly, free tier covers beta). **M1 interim (D37, not gated on this):** the invite flow ships complete behind an `EmailSender` seam with a dev/log implementation (local capture inbox in dev), so flipping the real vendor on is config + a ~20-line adapter whenever the account exists.
**RULED 2026-07-20 (Chris): no email vendor is chosen now.** M1 builds invite email-sending behind a seam — **interface only, no vendor binding** (D37 confirmed as recommended). The v1 minimum bar, which must work regardless of vendor: **the league join link is always visible and copyable by the league manager**, so they can paste it into any email/text themselves (tasks-M1 L.A2.5 updated). Vendor selection deferred until the invite-send feature is ready to ship mail. Recorded in spec **v2.8**.

**Q6 — three doc edits only you should approve (filed per C1/C2/C13, 2026-07-20).** (a) **CLAUDE.md Key Business Rule #5** says "Leagues are Pro only. Gate league creation **and joining**" — the spec says joining is free (×3); any session following rule 5 literally would Pro-gate the join route and break §7.2. Edit to: "League **creation** is Pro only; joining is free." (b) **CLAUDE.md Redesign section** still says league/team/live-draft screens are "UI-only… do not invent API routes or schema" — stale since the epic was greenlit (M0 shipped schema; M1 ships routes). Scope it to non-greenlit surfaces or delete. (c) **spec-leagues-live-mode.md** contradicts the epic (join Pro-only, `commissioner_id`, thin schema) and §2.3 formally supersedes it, but the file carries no banner saying so — add one pointing at spec-redraft-leagues.md. **Recommendation:** all three are one-line edits; say the word and the next session applies them verbatim. M1 builds to the spec meanwhile.
**RULED 2026-07-20 (Chris), item (a) — and wider: NEITHER creating NOR joining a league requires Pro.** Supersedes §7.2's "Create league (Pro only)" and CLAUDE.md Business Rule #5, **rewritten this session** to: "League creation and joining are free. Pro-level league features come later." Spec swept in **v2.8** (§3.1 goal 6, §7.2, §15.1, §16 preamble, §17 matrix, §19.1, §20 metric retired, Appendix C L.A1); tasks-M1 de-Pro'd — **DoD changes flagged in-task**: L.A1.12 (requireProUser gate + in-body is_pro check + the non-Pro 402 `PRO_REQUIRED` test all deleted; replaced by "a free user creates successfully") and L.A2.1 (Pro upsell/402 state removed from the wizard). Items (b) CLAUDE.md redesign bullet and (c) spec-leagues-live-mode superseded banner were NOT ruled and stay open under the tasks-doc conflict report (C2/C13).

**Q3 — split D/ST model (`dst_model = 'split'`, ESPN): no canonical core_box stat keys (filed per R8, 2026-07-19).** The ESPN parity templates — 2 of the 6 v1 templates — score D/ST as "split +5 pts / +5 yds (start +10)" (spec App B comparison table; `dst_model = 'split'` per the App B kicking/D-ST note), which needs points-allowed *and yards-allowed* tier-indicator keys. The registry has zero yards-allowed keys and the spec names no canonical split-model keys (B.4 names only the single-model `def_pa_*` family). D21 covered middle-tier naming for the single model only; the Q2/v2.7 punt covers B.2–B.3, not this core_box gap. Not an M0 blocker, but M1's ESPN template-parity work lands on it. **Recommendation:** extend the D21 convention — keep `def_pa_<lo>_<hi>` as the shared points side and add a `def_ya_<lo>_<hi>` yards-allowed family on ESPN's own bucket boundaries (confirm the exact buckets from ESPN's published table when building the M1 parity fixtures); registry is additive, so the keys land with M1's template work once blessed. Needs Chris/Architect sign-off on the naming before M1 seeds them.
**RESOLVED 2026-07-19 (Chris): approved as recommended.** The ESPN split-D/ST model gets registry keys named `def_ya_<lo>_<hi>`, per the D21 convention and Appendix B.4; `def_pa_<lo>_<hi>` stays the shared points side. Exact ESPN bucket boundaries confirmed from ESPN's published table when M1 builds the parity fixtures. Seeding the actual keys belongs to whichever task next touches the stat-key registry (registry is additive) — resolution recorded here, no keys seeded with it. Fold into the spec changelog when the keys land.

**Q1 — `sleeper_free` can't meet the live-provider hard requirements (kickoff timestamps + official inactives).** Spec §23.1 makes official inactives ~90 min pre-kickoff and in-game injury designations a hard requirement of whichever *live* provider runs, and §23.3 derives every lock from `nfl_games.kickoff_at` at evaluation time — but the free Sleeper schedule endpoint is day-granularity only, there is no official-inactives feed in the current integration, and nothing writes `nfl_games`.
**RESOLVED 2026-07-18 (Chris): proceed with nflverse.** `sleeper_free` stays the stats source; nflverse supplements kickoff timestamps (and is the likely official-inactives source). Engineering notes (D16): the nflverse adapter lands with the first runtime consumer of kickoffs (locks/schedule milestone — not M0); nflverse schedule/inactives data is retroactively fetchable, so the 2026 fixture library can be back-filled at any time — accepted limitation: back-fill loses real *arrival timing* of the ~90-min-pre-kickoff inactives publication (synthetic scenarios cover that timing per §23.1). M0's recorder proceeds as specced.

**Q2 — canonical advanced stat-key names: §23.5 vs Appendix B conflict.** §7.3.3's dot-product requires one shared key namespace, but §23.5 says `air_yards`/`yards_after_catch` while Appendix B.2 scores `receiving_air_yards`/`receiving_yac`.
**RESOLVED 2026-07-18 (Chris): punted — the named advanced stats were examples, not v1 commitments.** Misunderstanding corrected: air yards / YAC / yards-after-contact in §23.5/Appendix B are **illustrative** of the long-run advanced-scoring direction, contingent on raising money and funding a paid real-time stats API (or building one). No canonical naming decision is needed now. Consequences: M0 seeds `STAT_KEYS` with `core_box` only; the `tracking`/`charted` **tier machinery** (capability tiers, two-phase settle, revision handling — the §7.3.3 extensibility contract, which *is* v1-committed) is proven with clearly-marked placeholder keys (D15); real keys become a one-PR data task when funded (§23.5 checklist). Recorded in spec changelog **v2.7**; M1's Alpha/Ultra backtest exit criterion deferred (delivery plan **v1.3**). **Follow-up resolved same day (Chris): the v1 template picker ships the 6 parity templates only** — no Alpha/Ultra teaser cards; the FieldScout cards and the "same game, scored three ways" widget return when advanced stats are funded (spec §7.3.3/§16/App B/App C annotated).

## 4. Decisions log

*Build-time engineering decisions made during implementation that aren't spelled out verbatim in the spec — naming, library choices, minor sequencing calls. Product/scope decisions belong in the spec's own changelog (see spec-redraft-leagues.md), not here; this log is for "how we built it," not "what we're building."*

**2026-08-05 — Builder (L.B1.7):**
- **D111** Migration 072 mechanics (completion → `league_rosters` + `in_season` + `set_team_autodraft`; §8.5 step 6, §12.7, §12.2, §12.14, §8.4/§8.7, E48; D88/D96/D97 implemented; 066 amended in place, F12). (1) **The completion arm lives in `draft_apply_pick_internal`** (066, amended — not a new function): manual picks, tick autopicks, and the commissioner force path all write through that one internal, so completion is identical for every finisher (D90's one-advance-path doctrine extended to the finish line). plpgsql late binding makes 066's reference to a table created in 072 legal (function bodies resolve tables at execution; nothing executes the internal mid-chain). (2) **Lock/deadlock analysis (the completion txn updates leagues under the held drafts lock):** drafts→leagues is draft_reset's established order (069/R122-inverse). No cycle with draft_start's leagues-first order because under a live draft its idempotent arm returns off a PLAIN drafts read without ever taking the drafts lock (066's R122 block), and post-completion the league is no longer 'scheduled' so start refuses before its drafts FOR UPDATE; the picks INSERT's RI FOR KEY SHARE on leagues doesn't conflict with the UPDATE's FOR NO KEY UPDATE class. (3) **Roster row shape:** `acquisition_type='draft'`, `acquisition_cost` explicitly NULL for snake (D88 — the §12.7 DEFAULT 0 applies only when omitted; M3's auction writes `price` here), `slot_key`/IR columns NULL (M4's lineup engine owns slot assignment — drafting into slots is exactly the unsatisfiable-IR problem D91 excluded). A duplicate (league, player) at completion would mean engine corruption (`uniq_draft_player_live` makes it unreachable via any RPC path) — the §12.7 UNIQUE aborts LOUDLY, deliberately unconverted (no friendly message for an impossible state). (4) **The 3b broadcast trigger:** INSERT+UPDATE (the 070 draft_picks event shape) — M2's only writer is the completion INSERT; M4's add/drop/trade writers extend the trigger (and add DELETE) when they land, their milestone owning its surface (the D109(5) class, deliberately NOT an F-row; recorded here so it isn't a forgotten promise). Payload = {id, team_id, player_id, slot_key, acquisition_type, acquired_at}; excluded: league_id (topic-derivable), acquisition_cost (M3 renders spend), ir_placed_week/ir_lock_until_week (M4's IR surface). Broadcast burst residual: completion emits one event per roster row (≤ 240 at v1 max, sub-ms sends) once per league lifetime — clients are cache-hint consumers (§9); the leagues in_season UPDATE rides 070's existing status-column trigger for the home-hero flip. (5) **`set_team_autodraft` semantics (decided within the task's latitude):** "self" = the seat's CURRENT manager per the §12.2 league_members cache (the open-stint truth, v2.1) — a commissioner toggling their OWN seat is the self path (no override → no post); the D97 post fires only when actor ≠ seat manager AND a live/paused non-mock draft exists ("when a live draft exists" read as the room existing to see the post — paused included, the room is still open; message posts to `draft:<id>` context with the acting commissioner as author, the 069 style). §4.6 honored with a conditional lock: the live/paused non-mock drafts row (partial-unique-guaranteed singular) is locked FOR UPDATE before the member write, serializing the flag flip + post against the tick's ARM-2 `is_autodraft` read and in-flight picks (drafts→league_members = the tick's own order); with no such draft there is nothing to serialize against (the column has no reader outside the draft engine) and no lock is taken. Mocks excluded from the scan by construction (068 hard-codes `is_autodraft=FALSE` for mock seats — D110(4)). D63 no-op (same value) returns changed=false, posts nothing, holds no surprises for a double-tap. No action_id arm: the toggle is idempotent BY VALUE (replaying "on" is the D63 no-op) — the E2 ledger exists for non-idempotent mutations; recorded, not an oversight. (6) **E48 needed zero new code** — the task's item 4 is a composition proof: 063's vacate (no status gate — a league-lock affair), 068's no-user classification (ARM 2 autopicks a NULL-user seat AT the deadline, no grace — the hold is for humans), 062's status-unrestricted seat-targeted claim (fills the vacated member row, opens the stint), 066's turn check reading the refreshed cache. 026 §F drives the whole chain through the real RPCs, with the 1s-past-deadline discriminator separating "no grace" from the 30s human hold. (7) **Cross-reference pins flipped as promised** (the F12 discipline): 020's "league stays drafting" completion pin → 'in_season'; `draft-tick-db.test.ts`'s wire pin → in_season + roster assertions; 025's league_rosters table-ABSENCE diff cell → a rows-still-zero pin (its own text scheduled the upgrade — D110(8) discharged). (8) Typegen re-run (`--local`): **+75/−0** by `--numstat` (the league_rosters table type + relationships, `league_roster_broadcast_payload`, `set_team_autodraft`); alias block byte-identical + `LeagueRoster` added.

**2026-08-04 — Builder (L.B1.6):**
- **D110** Migration 071 mechanics (Mock Draft Mode — §8.8/§22.5/E59/E60; D93/D95/D102/D103 implemented; 065/066/068/069 amended in place, F12). (1) **The launcher control surface (the one decide-and-record beyond the task's printed list, folded as spec v2.8.16):** §8.8 names pause/resume in "Same engine, literally" and E59 requires resumability, but 069's pause/resume were commissioner-gated — a non-commissioner launcher's auto-paused mock would DEAD-END. `draft_pause`/`draft_resume` gained a mock-launcher arm (on `is_mock`, the only legal human caller is `config.mock.launched_by`; commissioners refused — D103(2)'s no-other-driver doctrine; the 42501 no-leak floor and message unchanged for real drafts, 023 untouched). Every OTHER §8.7 control refuses mocks with one friendly P0001: they are interference with a solo practice, and `draft_reset` was an outright zero-side-effect breach — LIVE-PROVEN pre-guard (main's unguarded draft_reset restored in a rolled-back txn: reset(mock) flipped the league `setup`→`scheduled` AND removed `settings.draft.draft_scheduled_at`), then closed + pinned. A launcher without the commissioner role gets 42501 on those seven (the controls simply don't exist for mocks — recorded). (2) **ARM placement + lock scope (the task charge):** ARM 1.6 (E59 stale-pause) runs BEFORE ARM 2 — a seat stale as-of-deadline last beat ≥ 45s before it, so the pause threshold (beat + grace + 5s) expires ≥ 40s before the grace hold (deadline + grace) does: the disconnected human can never lose their pick to the hold's expiry, by construction. ARM 2.5 (CPU think-time) runs AFTER ARM 2 (a deadline-expired mock CPU seat is ARM 2's immediate no-user autopick; the fresh deadline it writes puts the next think-due in the future) and BEFORE ARM 3 (the fresh deadline beats in the same pass). BOTH new claims carry the full body predicate in the WHERE (R135/R141: a healthy mock — fresh launcher, thinking CPU — is NEVER locked by any arm; the deleted-league EXISTS mirrored everywhere). One CPU pick per mock per pass (the think origin is the advance instant): `fast` ≈ 2s lands on the first tick after it elapses — effective cadence bounded by the 5s tick, the D87 granularity class, recorded residual. Untimed mocks: no clock to take a fraction of — CPUs think ~2s regardless of speed, pick start = `updated_at` (the advance instant), recorded. (3) **The PRNG:** `draft_mock_think_fraction` = 0.20 + 0.50 × (24 bits of md5(draft_id ‖ ':' ‖ pick) / 2^24), round 6 — provably non-negative ('00' + 6 hex), IMMUTABLE, stored-literal-pinned; near-buzzer picks fall out of the tail per the recorded contract. `draft_mock_cpu_due` is the ONE due computation (claim WHERE ≡ body re-check). Both defined in 068 (not 071) so a cron tick firing mid-reset never references a missing function — the 068→069 forward-reference lesson applied proactively; `draft_pause_internal` remains the one legal forward reference (no live mock can exist in the 068→071 window: create_mock_draft is 071's). (4) **Mock-aware resolution (D93's two consumers, one brain):** in `draft_autopick_resolve`, the human seat maps to the LAUNCHER (their queue/boards drive the seat's autopick — the chosen seat may be a placeholder or another member's franchise) and every CPU seat maps to NO USER (ADP + need — §8.8's bot behavior; a CPU seat never reads its real owner's queue/boards, pinned discriminatively: T1's CPU pick ignores u01's rb-topped primary board). ARM 2's mock branch mirrors the mapping and zeroes `is_autodraft` (seat-owner settings are irrelevant inside a practice room). `launched_by` is RPC-written (auth.uid()::text) so its ::uuid cast is safe; hand-crafted garbage is contained by the per-draft subtransaction. (5) **Order snapshot through the ONE resolution:** `draft_resolve_order_internal` extracted from `draft_start_internal` VERBATIM (066 amended; p_label preserves every pinned message; behavior byte-identical for the real path — 020's order pins prove it). The mock's candidate = the real scheduled draft row's stored order (the lobby order — D101), config fallback manual/custom-only (R123/R126 carried), seed = the MOCK's own id (a never-randomized `random` league gets a fresh deterministic shuffle per mock — the real order doesn't exist yet, the shuffle is the honest simulation, recorded). (6) **Caps (§22.5, in-body):** 3 active (live|paused; complete frees the slot — pinned) + 5 creations/hour, both per-user ACROSS leagues, serialized by `pg_advisory_xact_lock(hashtextextended('create_mock_draft:' ‖ uid))` — an advisory lock deliberately, never a profiles-row lock (profiles rows sit in the FK KEY-SHARE graph every chat INSERT touches — the R122 deadlock class; advisory locks live outside it). RECORDED RESIDUAL (F41 annotated): the hourly count is over SURVIVING rows — create→delete cycles evade it; the route-layer backstop is M7's existing F41 charter. Cap refusals are discriminatingly pinned (the 4th-active fires with 3-in-hour; the 6th-in-hour fires with 2 actives; the 60-min boundary both ways). (7) **Child cleanup + the idle definition (task rules, decided):** delete/expiry ride CASCADE for picks/queues/liveness and EXPLICITLY delete `league_chat` rows where context = 'draft:<mock_id>' (the chat FK is to the LEAGUE — CASCADE can't reach them; leaving them strands a member-visible ghost context against E59's "zero league impact"; D99's append-only bans client delete POLICIES — this is the server erasing a deleted room, not an edit surface; the league's own context pinned untouched). Expiry idle_since = GREATEST(drafts.updated_at, the launcher's last beat) — either engine activity or human presence resets the clock (the conservative deletion posture; the E59 pause freezes updated_at within ~35s of abandonment, so the 72h clock effectively starts at abandonment); launch seeds the launcher's beat so a row always exists (COALESCE started_at/created_at guards fixture mocks). Completed recaps NEVER expire (owner-delete only); mocks under soft-deleted leagues DO expire when incomplete (the F50 zombie class gets that much cleanup free — completed recaps under deleted leagues linger, recorded residual). (8) **The zero-side-effect diff pin's league_rosters cell** is a table-ABSENCE pin (the table is 072's — no roster write is even possible today); 072's completion arm must keep its mock bypass and re-pins from the completion side per its own task text. (9) **020's seam pin flipped** to the launcher refusal (the fixture mock is config-less → launched_by NULL → tick-only, the safe default for privileged-insert shapes — every RPC writer stamps config.mock). (10) Typegen re-run (`--local`; six functions, additive-only; alias block preserved byte-identical). ATTESTATION CORRECTED (batch 7, R152): the launch commit's diff was **+37/−0**, not the attested "+36" — a stray blank line the +-line grep missed; `--numstat` is the honest counter. Post-R149 signature re-run: **+38/−0 vs main** (+1 vs the launch commit — `p_action_id?: string`). (11) **Launch idempotency (batch 7, R149):** §4 rule 6 (E2) requires action_id on every client-triggered mutation — the launch shipped without one and the double-tap residual was recorded nowhere (the R51/R118 untracked-promise class). Closed in-body, the stronger arm: `p_action_id uuid DEFAULT NULL`; the replay lookup runs AFTER the advisory-lock serializer (a concurrent double-tap serializes into create-then-replay) and BEFORE league/cap validation (the same intent must not trip caps its own creation already passed — pinned at both caps' ceilings), returning the ORIGINAL mock with `created: false` (the 060 replay pattern). Ledger = `config.mock.action_id` (no schema change), stamped only when the route sends a key (the exact-object config pin proves the NULL path stamps none), matched launcher-scoped as TEXT (R117) — an action_id never replays across users (pinned: a foreign presenter falls through to their own cap refusal). NULL = no-dedupe legacy path (the §5 sketch's printed 3-arg call stands); the L.B2.3 route mints one UUID per submit and treats created=false as the same 2xx (read-list line extended). A DELETED mock does not replay — the row is the only ledger (the (6) surviving-rows posture; M7/F41 owns the route-layer backstop). v2.8.16 erratum completed in place (§8.8 config.mock shape + changelog, dated notes, no version bump — the R145 precedent). R121 stays routed (019 untouched — its queue-policy pins are name/cmd-level and hold across the carve-out; the behavior pins live in 025).

**2026-08-04 — Builder (L.B1.5):**
- **D109** Migration 070 mechanics (Broadcast-from-DB + channel auth — the repo's first realtime; §9.1–9.3, §12.14/D89, plan §8.4; standing rule 5 now literal). (1) **The send-vs-broadcast_changes adaptation (the task's sanctioned environment latitude, recorded):** `realtime.broadcast_changes()` (local signature verified live) broadcasts the WHOLE NEW/OLD rows — for these tables that is exactly the blind payload §9.2 bans (leagues carries invite_code + settings + scoring_rules_snapshot; drafts carries config) — so the trigger fns compose the SAME envelope broadcast_changes builds ({operation, table, schema, record}) with a COLUMN-SELECTED record from a per-table payload function and call `realtime.send()` directly (broadcast_changes is itself a thin wrapper over send; the observable contract — private-channel Broadcast-from-DB + policy auth — is preserved). `old_record` deliberately omitted (clients patch caches from `record`; §9.3's cache-hint semantics — no consumer renders pre-images). EVENT = the table name ('drafts' | 'draft_picks' | 'league_chat' | 'leagues'; the heartbeat is 'tick'), operation inside the payload — one topic multiplexes four sources (§5 inventory), so the event key is the client's dispatch discriminator (L.B3.1 subscribes per event). `realtime.send()` traps its own errors (verified: internal RAISE WARNING) — a realtime outage can never fail a pick/chat/settings transaction; it injects an 'id' key into payloads when absent (delivery bookkeeping — every stored-shape pin accounts for it). (2) **Payload functions are the testable unit** (pgTAP 024 pins exact key sets + named sensitive negatives): `draft_broadcast_payload` = the §5 inventory + `deadline_remaining_ms` — a recorded EXTENSION under §9.2's own rule ("broadcast only the columns clients render" — the §16.5.2 pause overlay renders it), `updated_at` = the D92 state_version, NEVER config/auction fields; `draft_pick_broadcast_payload` = exactly the §5 six, NEVER action_id (another client's idempotency key) / picked_by / made_via / price; `league_chat_broadcast_payload` = id (a list item needs its key — unlike the topic-identified singletons) / user_id / message / context / is_system / created_at; `league_broadcast_payload` = status/name/avatar_url ONLY (the §5 "only"; THE break-probe target). Payload fns are plain internals (062 form, triple-REVOKE) and STABLE not IMMUTABLE (jsonb timestamptz encoding reads the TimeZone GUC); trigger fns are SECURITY DEFINER `search_path=''` per the §9.2 printed pattern, triple-REVOKEd as hygiene (they fire as owner and are uncallable as triggers anyway). (3) **Trigger surface:** drafts AFTER UPDATE (every RPC bumps updated_at — D108(1) — so every update is a meaningful state_version event); draft_picks AFTER INSERT OR UPDATE (undo flips broadcast to the board); league_chat AFTER INSERT with topic = the context string itself when 'draft:%' (the §12.13 grammar IS the topic — 065's INSERT policy enforces it for client rows, 069's system posts write the same shape) else 'league:<league_id>'; leagues AFTER UPDATE **OF status, name, avatar_url** — column-TRIGGERED, not merely column-selected: a settings/lifecycle write touching nothing a client renders emits NOTHING (pinned discriminator; a same-value SET of a named column still fires — harmless under cache-hint semantics). AFTER-ROW cost under the held draft lock ≈ one jsonb build + one INSERT (~sub-ms); the §4.6 held-lock wire assertions (020/023 families) run with the triggers LIVE and stay green. (4) **Channel auth asymmetry (recorded):** the league SELECT policy is the §9.2 PRINTED SQL verbatim (split_part + ::uuid) — a malformed 'league:<not-a-uuid>' topic therefore ERRORS (22P02) at policy evaluation instead of matching zero rows; refusal-by-error, pinned honestly in 024. The draft SELECT policy (the spec's "equivalent policy via the drafts.league_id lookup" — shape unprinted, Builder's) uses the R117 exact-grammar text compare (`'draft:' \|\| d.id = topic`): no cast on hostile topics, and 'draft:<valid-id>:junk' matches NO draft (pinned) — the 065 chat-context lesson applied where latitude existed, the printed LAW form kept where it didn't. (5) **Presence-only INSERT policies shipped (recorded scope decision):** Realtime Authorization gates presence-track/client-send on INSERT policies over realtime.messages; §9's mechanism table + §9.1's channel inventory give members Presence on their topics (the §16.2 presence-bar is L.B3.1's, a UI task with no migration lane), and 070 is the channel-auth surface's only schema-lane home — so the INSERT pair ships here with `extension = 'presence'` + the same membership predicates. `extension = 'broadcast'` is DELIBERATELY excluded: client-broadcast write would let any member FORGE a server-shaped 'drafts'/'draft_picks' event to the whole room (spoofing the authoritative channel; §9.3's refetch-on-doubt limits but does not excuse it) — pinned refused for a member. §9's Client Broadcast row (typing, bid-button pulses) has NO M2 consumer; if M3's auction UX wants it, M3 extends the policy deliberately (recorded here, not an F-row — M3's own task list owns its surface, and nothing ships broken without it). (6) **The heartbeat vehicle (068 ARM 3, amended in place — F12; the task's explicit latitude, finalized):** one `realtime.send()` PER TICK PASS (~5s) per live draft under a non-deleted league — not every third pass: the tick is stateless across invocations (a 1-in-3 counter would need a persisted cell — a new write surface — or clock-modulo with cron-jitter gaps), a ≤5s beat strictly tightens §9.1's printed 15s correction cadence, and the cost (1 msg/5s per live draft) is bounded by the ≤25-draft M2 gate. Recorded residual: 3× the printed 15s message volume; M7's §22.6 load gate measures message volume under its existing charter — no new obligation. LOCK DISCIPLINE (the R135/R141 charge): the beat is a PLAIN SELECT — no FOR UPDATE, no claim, ZERO new lock scope (a healthy live draft stays untouched by the tick); it runs AFTER the state-changing arms so the beat reflects post-arm state (a just-made autopick's fresh deadline beats in the same pass). Paused drafts excluded (frozen clock — current_deadline NULL, the §16.5.2 display reads deadline_remaining_ms via refetch); deleted-league drafts excluded (the R141 mirror — F50's zombie class gets no beat); mocks (once 071 makes them reachable) beat like real drafts. Payload {server_now, current_deadline}; summary keys heartbeats/heartbeat_failures; the arm is exception-contained (send already traps — containment symmetry). (7) **D89 applied AND made falsifiable:** the §12.14 publication ALTER is NOT executed; §12.14 rewritten as the Broadcast-from-DB trigger inventory (spec → **v2.8.15**, the fold-back rule; flagged again for Chris in the PR body per D89); pgTAP 024 pins `supabase_realtime` carries NONE of the league/draft tables. (8) **F8 flipped with the full D38-banner inventory** (§6 — every waivered table dispositioned: trigger in 070, F42 to M4, or never-broadcast by doctrine); F42's six tables verified AND pinned trigger-less (024 §A) so a drive-by trigger cannot land unnoticed. (9) **Wire proof over the REAL Realtime service** (`draft-realtime-db.test.ts`, 2): a member supabase-js client subscribes the PRIVATE `draft:<id>` channel (explicit realtime.setAuth — deterministic auth), the tick's autopick INSERT arrives with EXACTLY the six selected columns + the drafts advance (8 keys, no config) + the 'tick' heartbeat; a non-member's subscribe never reaches SUBSCRIBED and delivers ZERO events while a fresh member channel hears the cron heartbeat in the same window (the positive control — silence proven against a delivering room). One channel per topic per client (supabase-js constraint, discovered live) — test (a) releases its topic in a `finally` so a failure can never cascade into test (b), the §9.3 discipline in miniature. BOOT-RACE FINDING (observed live, twice, under the full parallel `npm run test` immediately after a `db reset`): the restarted realtime container accepts private-channel JOINS before Broadcast-from-DB DELIVERY flows — it recreates the realtime.messages day partitions at boot and `realtime.send()` SILENTLY drops inserts until they exist (its internal trap), so trigger-emitted messages are LOST, not late. The suite's beforeAll therefore carries a DELIVERY-proving readiness gate (subscribe the league topic, fire the leagues trigger via a same-value name UPDATE — UPDATE OF fires on the SET list — until an event actually arrives; retried ≤ 8×; a dead service still fails loudly, never a skip); two consecutive reset→full-run sequences green post-gate. The same partition race is guarded in pgTAP 024 by an in-txn create-if-missing partition DO-block (rolled back). PRODUCTION posture: not a production defect — hosted realtime restarts are platform-managed and the drop window is boot-scoped; L.B3.1's client rules (§9.3 fetch-then-subscribe + gap⇒refetch) are the by-design recovery for any lost broadcast. (10) Typegen re-run (`--local`): +17 lines, the four payload fns (composite-arg Row types); trigger-returning fns not emitted; alias block preserved byte-identical, diff additive-only.

**2026-08-04 — Builder (L.B1.4):**
- **D108** Migration 069 mechanics (§8.7 commissioner controls + system posts + the outage arm; every RPC SECURITY DEFINER `search_path=''` + in-body auth + REVOKE FROM PUBLIC, anon per §4.1; `draft_pause_internal` plain + triple-revoked, the 062/068 internal form). (1) **Auth/lock shape:** every control locks the DRAFTS row FIRST (§4.6), then answers ONE no-leak 42501 for {nonexistent draft, non-member, non-commish} via `is_league_commish` (co-commissioners pass — §17). The league row is NOT locked outside reset (the R122 lesson); a role demotion racing a control = at most one in-flight control under the old role (the 066 removal-race class, recorded not "fixed"). Every control bumps `drafts.updated_at` (the D92 state_version). (2) **Pause bookkeeping = ONE implementation** (`draft_pause_internal`; consumers: the RPC + the tick's outage arm): remaining = round(ms to `current_deadline`) — MAY BE NEGATIVE (a pause during a D102 grace hold preserves the elapsed hold; "clocks never gain/lose" applied to the grace clock too; the R132 as-of-deadline window shifts with the restored deadline and both directions come out right — a seat that beat during the pause is connected ⇒ §8.5.4 autopick, a silent seat serves the REMAINDER of its hold; *negative remaining PINNED ms-exact (−61000) at 023 §H — added M2 batch 5, R136*); `current_deadline` NULLed while paused (the persisted column is the §16.5.2 display source; the tick claims `status='live'` only); resume = now() + remaining × `interval '1 millisecond'` (exact integer-ms math, never float seconds); untimed drafts round-trip NULL. Double-pause/double-resume = D63 no-ops (`paused|resumed: false`) posting nothing. (3) **Clock edit (E15) exact semantics:** the new timer writes `drafts.config.pick_timer_seconds` — the room's POST-start authority (D95: league settings are the pre-start store; league settings untouched) — and governs subsequent picks mechanically; `p_extend_current` (live only): deadline := GREATEST(current, now()+timer) — extend-only, a shorter timer never silently shortens; timer 0 + extend ⇒ deadline NULL (§8.2 untimed applied now — removing a clock is not shortening it); *UNTIMED current + extend (R138, M2 batch 5 — decided KEEP + pin): GREATEST(NULL, now()+timer) IMPOSES the new timer on the current pick — nominally the one finite-ward move under extend-only, but it is the commissioner's EXPLICIT request (extend_current with a positive timer on an untimed pick has exactly one meaning: put this pick on the clock), the §8.2-symmetric inverse of the timer-0 arm, grants a FULL fresh timer (no running countdown is ever cut — the harm E15 guards), and refusing would leave a stalled untimed room no recourse short of force-pick; the system post says "The current pick is now on the clock." for this arm (vs "was extended.") — deadline + message pinned, 023 §E*; paused + extend → friendly refusal (resume first — extending a frozen clock would rewrite the bookkeeping); catalog membership is the route's Zod (L.B2.3). (4) **Undo semantics + THE R125 DECISION:** p_to NULL = single (highest live pick), p_to = N = cascade keeping 1..N (E4's "undo pick #40" ≡ p_to 39); soft `is_undone` (§12.4 audit), pool return = the partial index, rewind to the first reverted number with a FRESH full clock (live: now()+timer; paused: remaining := timer×1000, deadline stays NULL); `complete` → F44-class refusal naming M6 (undo would be the same backward move as post-completion reset). R125 decided: **an action_id is consumed forever** — the E2 replay arm keeps NO `is_undone` filter, so a stale retry of an undone pick is a success-shaped historical no-op that can never re-apply the pick (filtering would 23505 on `uniq_draft_action` — undone rows keep their action_id — surfaced as a misleading E1 message); pinned in 023 at the strongest discriminator (caller on clock + player available + nothing written); the batch-2 findings row flipped resolved. (5) **Reassign/move validation:** exclusivity refusals name the player ("already on a roster in this draft" / "already on that team" — replays surface as these refusals, safe by state, never double-applied); slot validation = CAPACITY (target's live picks < `total_rounds` — the v2.8.11 reading: need-fit is §8.4 autopick's, E16's bipartite is M4's); before/after in the post. (6) **Force pick:** the ONE advance path (`draft_apply_pick_internal`) with made_via='commissioner', picked_by = the commissioner, is_auto FALSE (a deliberate human action); OPTIONAL `p_action_id` (draft_picks has the column — the force button gets E2 idempotency for free, same R125 semantics; replay posts nothing); live only (paused → friendly refusal — apply-pick's now()-based deadline would corrupt the pause bookkeeping). (7) **Set order (E31):** permutation-validated (uuid[] typed at the boundary); `scheduled` writes the lobby order (draft_start honors the draft-row candidate, D101/R123 — L.B2.1's pre-start PATCH stays the ordinary surface, both writers legal pre-start); live/paused: completed picks untouched, the CURRENT pick's team re-derives (a CHANGED on-clock team gets a fresh full clock — it never had the clock; unchanged keeps its running clock), remaining picks re-derive mechanically (advance reads `drafts.draft_order`). RECORDED RESIDUAL: a mid-round edit can leave per-team totals uneven — E31 sanctions the edit as printed, §10.4's full-power philosophy owns it, completion counts the BOARD (066), the post is the transparency, F40's M6 audit row the record. (8) **Reset + THE D107(5) SEAM CLOSURE:** live/paused only (complete → F44 refusal; scheduled → friendly "has not started"); one txn: picks soft-undone, drafts → 'scheduled' (counters reset, clock fields + started_at/completed_at NULL, `draft_order` KEPT — D101, the shown order survives), this draft's `draft_liveness` DELETED (fresh room state; the outage arm's supervision memory starts clean), leagues.status='scheduled' (§8.7's own row; D43 indifferent — the snapshot survives) AND `settings.draft.draft_scheduled_at` REMOVED (`#-`) — a PAST instant would re-start the reset draft on the next 5s tick (D94); re-scheduling through the picker re-arms auto-start, the Start button needs no instant. Post-reset state (league 'scheduled' + draft 'scheduled' + no instant) is a state only reset produces; 059's setup→scheduled instant requirement untouched. LOCK ORDER: reset takes drafts → leagues — the R122-INVERSE, and the only control needing the leagues FOR UPDATE (taking leagues first would recreate the R122 FK-KEY-SHARE cycle against an in-flight pick); no cycle against 066's leagues → drafts direction because create/start reach their drafts lock only under a 'scheduled' league gate while reset runs only on live/paused drafts (which coexist only with 'drafting' leagues) — banner-stated. Every OTHER control's chat INSERT takes the same implicit leagues KEY SHARE as a pick (drafts → leagues(share), the direction every writer already follows). (9) **The outage arm (068 ARM 1.5, amended in place — F12):** pauses a live NON-mock draft when supervision was ESTABLISHED then LOST — ≥ 1 commissioner/co-commissioner `draft_liveness` row exists AND none newer than now() − (`draft_liveness_freshness()` + `disconnect_grace_seconds`). Freshness is AS-OF-NOW — the deliberate contrast with R132's as-of-deadline pick hold: the hold classifies a seat at a FIXED past instant a later beat must not rewrite; the outage check is a CONTINUOUS present-tense supervision question with no reference instant (a returning commissioner ends the condition immediately but NEVER auto-resumes — resume is a commissioner action, pinned). NEVER-CONNECTED EXEMPTION: zero commissioner liveness rows = D94's unattended autopilot, never paused (§8.7's "disconnects" presupposes a connection; pausing never-attended drafts would dead-end every D94 auto-start — "absent rooms still draft correctly"). Runs BETWEEN auto-start and timeout so an expired deadline under an outage PAUSES instead of autopicking (*PINNED, M2 batch 5 R136: deadline planted 61s past — beyond grace, so a first-running timeout arm would autopick unconditionally — → paused, −61000 persisted, ZERO picks; break probe: arms physically swapped → exactly the two pins RED (`paused|30000` + 1 pick) → reverted*); *CLAIM SCOPE (R135, M2 batch 5; corrected by R141, batch-5 addendum): the claim's WHERE carries the same established-then-lost supervision predicate the body re-verifies under the lock, PLUS the body's `leagues.deleted_at` re-check mirrored in (R141 — without it a live draft under a soft-deleted league, reachable today because `soft_delete_league` (060) has no draft-status gate, matched the claim WHERE FOREVER: claimed + locked every tick, body-skipped, never paused, never leaving the candidate set — a permanent-candidate class that falsified the recorded no-loop rationale; pinned in 023 §H via pgrowlocks on the LD fixture — no "For Update" + still live), plus LIMIT 25 (ARM 2 symmetry; an outage pause is idempotent and not deadline-urgent — but "overflow candidates wait ≤5s" holds only INSIDE the ≤25-draft M2 gate: the claim has no ORDER BY/loop/v_seen, so beyond the gate nothing rotates the batch and candidates that never leave the set — e.g. persistent body failures — could starve real outage candidates, whose due deadlines ARM 2 then autopicks in the SAME tick; routed to M7/L.F1's §22.6 load gate as **F50**) — pre-fix the unfiltered claim FOR-UPDATE-locked EVERY live non-mock draft each tick and held the locks for the rest of the tick txn (live two-session probe: a pick RPC on a fully supervised, not-due draft died on lock_timeout behind the batch — against §22.3/§22.6 and the 25-draft M2 gate); pinned via pgrowlocks lock-visibility (023 §H: no "For Update" on a supervised (LP) or never-connected (LN) fixture after the ticks — tuple locks carry forward through same-txn updates, so the negative probes are rows no legitimate claim ever touched; a claimed-and-body-skipped due draft is the probe-validity positive control; break probe: filter dropped → both negative pins RED → reverted); a filter bug that under-claims would miss the pause — §H's 76s-threshold pins guard that direction*; per-draft subtransactions; summary keys `outage_paused`/`outage_failures`; the 068→069 forward reference to `draft_pause_internal` is call-time-resolved and unreachable in the reset window (banner-noted). Spec § 8.7 erratum folded → **v2.8.14**. (10) **`league_chat.user_id` DROP NOT NULL:** the tick's outage post has no acting user (user_id NULL = "the system"); commissioner-action posts carry the ACTING commissioner's user_id + `is_system=TRUE`; no client hole opened (the 065 INSERT policy pins `user_id = auth.uid()` — NULL never passes; pinned). Messages are UX and pgTAP-pinned exactly; actor named via `draft_actor_name()` (COALESCE(display_name, username); broad-EXECUTE pure helper, the draft_team_for_pick breadth precedent). (11) **`p_reason`:** accepted on every control (TEXT DEFAULT NULL, the 063 form), Zod-validated at L.B2.3's routes, STORED NOWHERE — deliberately not interpolated into the chat post either (the post is the ACTION's transparency; the reason's home is M6's `commissioner_actions`, F40). (12) **Idempotency classes per control (recorded):** pause/resume = state no-ops; force = optional action_id replay; undo/reassign/move/set_order/reset = state-guarded friendly refusals or natural-target semantics — no replay can double-apply any control; full replay-ledger idempotency needs M6's audit table. (13) pgTAP 023 message pins are exact-message COUNT pins, not "latest post" reads — `created_at` is txn-frozen in pgTAP and `id` is a random UUID, so insertion order is unrecoverable in-txn (a harness fact, not a product one). (14) Typegen re-run (`--local`; 9 RPCs + `draft_pause_internal` + `draft_actor_name` + the user_id nullability — the one deliberate loosening; alias block preserved byte-identical). Wire-suite action-id prefix `ad400000` (ad0/ad1/ad2 taken by the sibling suites — committed wire leagues persist between runs and `create_league`'s idempotency key is globally unique). (15) **`league_chat.user_id` FK → ON DELETE SET NULL (R137, M2 batch 5; 069 amended in place — F12):** 001's CASCADE meant deleting the acting commissioner's account hard-deleted their system posts — against §12.13's "(non-deletable)" system posts, D99's append-only chat, and D97's interim-audit purpose. SET NULL applies to ALL chat rows DELIBERATELY: a plain FK cannot be partial, splitting system from ordinary rows would need a trigger for no principled gain, and the semantic is coherent — account deletion removes the IDENTITY, not the room's history (the same rule M1's membership work applies to the teams/picks a departed member leaves behind; D99 already forbids every chat delete path, and the M6 audit table (F40) will make the same authorship-outlives-account choice). An authorless ordinary message renders by its `is_system=FALSE` shape (the "former member" fallback is L.B3.3's chat UI); `user_id` NULL was already a legal, forge-proof shape post-069 (the 065 INSERT policy pins `user_id = auth.uid()`). Pinned (023 §I): form pin `confdeltype='n'` + both shapes survive the author's `auth.users` deletion with text intact. Residual, recorded not routed: a deleted user's message TEXT persists — content-level erasure is a product/GDPR question no current feature owns; if account deletion ever becomes a product surface, it must decide message-content policy then (the identity link is already severed here).
