**2026-08-03 — Builder (L.B4.1):**
- **D106** Migration 067 mechanics (`league_lists` + the §15.5 surface; NO SECURITY DEFINER routine created — the 065/D104 shape, so no REVOKE owed under §4.1; §12.15's owner-scoped FOR ALL is the spec-named client-write exception per plan §8.2, so writes are direct table DML with RLS as the enforcement backstop and the routes' job is friendly 4xxs). (1) **The ownership invariant made a constraint (the R43 comment-becomes-constraint class):** §12.15's `owner_id` comment ("matches lists.owner_id") had no printed enforcement — a member could INSERT `owner_id = self` + `list_id = someone else's list` (passing the FOR ALL WITH CHECK) and with `shared_with_league = TRUE` force-share the victim's PRIVATE list to the whole league. Closed declaratively: composite FK `(list_id, owner_id) → lists(id, owner_id)` backed by `UNIQUE (id, owner_id)` on lists (trivially satisfied — id is the PK); 23503 on every path, pinned privileged AND from the authenticated role in pgTAP 021; folded into the LAW as spec **v2.8.12** with the companion-policy erratum. (2) **The shared-private read ships for `lists` AND `list_players`:** the §12.15 note prints only the `lists` policy, but its stated purpose ("let members OPEN a shared-but-private list") is unreachable without the rows — 001's `list_players` policy re-states list visibility inline, so it does not follow the new lists policy automatically. Both additive policies carry `deleted_at IS NULL` (owner-only read of soft-deleted lists, mirroring 001's public arm); the four 001 lists/list_players policies untouched (policies_are pins the exact 3-policy sets both tables). (3) **Defense in depth, probed live BOTH directions:** the content policies' EXISTS subqueries run under `league_lists`'s own RLS, so dropping `shared_with_league = TRUE` from the `lists` policy ALONE is masked (021 stayed green — first probe attempt), while dropping it from the `league_lists` SELECT policy fails exactly the link-visibility pins (tests 28/36/37 — the shown break probe); a non-shared CONTENT leak requires BOTH layers to loosen. Recorded in the 021 header's falsifiability map so no future attestation claims single-conjunct falsifiability of the content pins (the R84/R97 lesson applied proactively — the header's draft claim was corrected when the first probe refuted it). (4) `idx_league_lists_list` added beside the printed indexes (§8.1: the shared-read policies probe by `list_id`; the printed index covers `(league_id, owner_id)` only). (5) **Where each check lives (the routed decide-and-record):** membership → route probe on `league_members` (no-leak 404, same answer as nonexistent league) backstopped by WITH CHECK `is_league_member` (42501); list ownership → route probe on `lists` via RLS (invisible → 404 no-leak; visible-but-foreign → friendly 403) backstopped by the composite FK (23503); soft-deleted list → 404 at attach; already-attached → route probe on the natural key `(league_id, list_id, owner_id)` BEFORE the clear-first side effect *(added in the batch-3 fix cycle, R127 — the 23505 at the INSERT remains the race backstop)*; the FK backstop's mapped answer is 403 ≡ the probe path's for the same condition *(corrected from 400 in the batch-3 fix cycle, R128)*. BOTH write paths probe BEFORE the clear-first side effect: PATCH probes the target row — the SELECT policy also shows other members' shared rows, so promoting a foreign/missing llid must 404 without demoting the caller's own primary — and attach probes the natural key (a 409 must carry zero side effects); both orderings pinned in the stack suite; "not yours" ≡ "not found" (no-leak). (6) **Primary-switch semantics:** the route clears the caller's current primary first (§7.4's one-tap switch) with the target excluded by id (PATCH) or by list_id (attach) so REPLAYS are retry-safe — a replayed attach-as-primary 409s WITHOUT demoting the primary its original call set (DoD idempotency). *(Corrected in the batch-3 fix cycle, R127: the 409 comes from the pre-side-effect natural-key probe, not the 23505 — the exclusion alone only covered the byte-identical replay; the neighboring attach-as-primary of a list originally attached NON-primary ran the clear-first demote and then 409'd on the natural key, leaving the caller with ZERO primary under an error response. Post-fix the probe answers the sequential/visible already-attached cases pre-demote (a mixed-flag concurrent twin can still race the window — R131); the `.neq list_id` exclusion is a race backstop only.)* `uniq_primary_board_per_member` remains the guarantee — a lost race is a friendly 409 (`PRIMARY_RACE_MESSAGE`), a replayed attach a friendly 409 (`ALREADY_ATTACHED_MESSAGE`), never two primaries (021 pins the 23505 at the index from the owner's own client UPDATE). (7) **PostgREST embed FK-hinted:** the composite FK gives league_lists TWO relationships to lists → a bare `lists(...)` embed is PGRST201-ambiguous; the service embeds `lists!league_lists_list_id_fkey(...)` (response key stays `lists`). GET keeps null-embed rows (a shared attachment of a since-deleted list stays member-visible as a ROW — content unreadable — so the owner's panel can surface it for detach). (8) **Rule-5 disposition:** league_lists has NO live subscriber anywhere in M2 (the L.B4.2 panel is query-invalidated; room liveness rides the draft:<id> picks channel per E17/tasks-M2 §5's channel inventory, which omits league_lists) — no broadcast trigger owed; **F42 appended** with league_lists so a future subscriber's milestone finds the obligation (R51). (9) Reads: the hook consumes the §15.5 GET route (one implementation of the mine+shared join with the embed) rather than a direct table SELECT — D92's "reads are RLS-scoped hooks" is satisfied THROUGH the route since RLS scopes the rows either way and the printed GET is the §15.5 contract; set-primary optimistic per §15.6 (cache flips at-most-one-primary scoped to the caller's own rows — a fellow member's shared attachment may be primary FOR THEM and must not flip; rollback on error, refetch on settle). (10) Typegen re-run (`--local`; new table + relationships, diff additive-only, alias block re-appended byte-identical + `LeagueList` added). **2026-08-03 — Builder (L.B1.2):**
- **D105** Migration 066 mechanics (the draft core; every RPC SECURITY DEFINER `search_path=''` + in-body auth + REVOKE FROM PUBLIC, anon per §4.1; SQLSTATE table carried verbatim from 062/063). (1) **Two pure helpers beside the RPCs:** `draft_rounds_from_roster(jsonb)` (D91's one implementation — starters + bench, IR excluded) and `draft_team_for_pick(order, type, reversal, pick)` (D90's one order-math implementation — linear repeats; snake parity by round; 3RR = parity inverted for rounds ≥ 3). Both IMMUTABLE, non-SECURITY-DEFINER, EXECUTE deliberately left broad (pure math over arguments, no data access — the `is_league_member` breadth precedent); 068's tick and 071's mock snapshot reuse them, and pgTAP 020 pins the tables BOTH directly at the helper and from real driven picks so the RPC wiring cannot drift. (2) **Order resolution at start (D101 refined; CORRECTED by R122-batch's R123, 2026-08-03):** the stored candidate is `drafts.draft_order` when present (a pre-start randomize/order-edit via L.B2.1's PATCH), else — in manual/custom mode ONLY — the re-hydrated config's `draft_order`; the draft-row value wins so the order the lobby displayed is the order that drafts. *(As originally shipped the config fallback also applied under `random`, where NO lobby ever displayed a config-sourced order — a stale settings-blob leftover from a prior manual/custom episode started VERBATIM, live-proven by the batch-2 review; the "lobby showed it" rationale below is true only of the draft-row candidate. Fixed: random reads only `drafts.draft_order` — R123, pinned with the LK stored-literal md5 fixture.)* `manual`/`custom` REQUIRE the candidate to be a permutation of the active (non-retired) team ids — length, distinct, membership, compared as TEXT so malformed entries fail validation, never a `::uuid` cast (the R117 lesson) — else a friendly P0001; `random` honors a VALID DRAFT-ROW candidate as-is (reshuffling at start would make the shown order a lie) and otherwise shuffles deterministically by `md5(draft_id || team_id)` (seed = the row's own id; no wall-clock, no Math.random anywhere in the engine). (3) **`draft_start` is create-if-absent** by composing the idempotent `draft_create` (D94's no-dead-end principle applied to the MANUAL path — a league scheduled purely through the settings surface has no drafts row and the Start button must still work); re-start of a live draft is a D63-class idempotent no-op (`started:false`, board untouched — pinned), and a live-draft-under-a-scheduled-league inconsistency refuses loudly rather than silently resetting a board. (4) **Seams as explicit refusals:** auction start AND auction pick refuse with friendly P0001s naming M3 (the 059 "lands in M2" precedent — M2 implements none of §8.6); `is_mock` picks refuse naming L.B1.6/071 so the human-turn path can never silently apply to mocks before the D103(2) branch is amended in (cross-referenced in the 066 banner and tasks-M2 L.B1.6). (5) **Lock order (CORRECTED by R122, batch 2 — the original "no cycle exists" clause missed the FK row lock):** `draft_create`/`draft_start` take leagues → drafts; `draft_make_pick` locks the drafts row explicitly and reads leagues/league_members/teams unlocked — BUT its draft_picks INSERT takes an implicit RI FOR KEY SHARE on the leagues row (the `league_id` FK, 065:152), so the pick's true order is drafts → leagues(KEY SHARE) and a drafts-row FOR UPDATE taken under the league lock while a pick is in flight DOES deadlock (live-proven 40P01 — the batch-2 probe; the victim can be the manager's pick). The discipline that prevents it (the R122 fix): draft_start's idempotent no-op arm reads the drafts row WITHOUT locking it, and the drafts-row FOR UPDATE is taken only BELOW the `scheduled` league gate, where no pick's INSERT can be in flight (picks require 'live'; start moves draft + league in one txn under the league lock) — structural prosrc pin in pgTAP 020. The league row stays explicitly unlocked in the pick path (heartbeats must not contend, D102; a removal racing a pick = at most one in-flight pick lands, identical to §7.2.1's removal-on-the-same-pick-clock semantics — recorded, not "fixed"). No commissioner bypass exists in `draft_make_pick` (pinned): the force path is L.B1.4's. (6) **E2 replay semantics:** the replay short-circuit runs BEFORE status/turn checks and returns `{draft, pick}` — the ORIGINAL pick row + the CURRENT authoritative draft state, with no outcome discriminator — so an immediate replay is byte-identical (pinned via GUC-captured text and wire deep-equal). **Completion counts live (non-undone) picks** rather than inferring from `current_pick_number` (robust against 069's undo rewinds) and is mock-sufficient per the task banner: `drafts.complete`/`completed_at` only, the league deliberately STAYS `drafting` — pgTAP pins that, and 072 flips the pin when the transition + `league_rosters` land. (7) **Slot-fit reading recorded:** need-fit/K-D/ST deferral are AUTOPICK-only rules (§8.4 frames them under "Autopick strategy"); a manual snake pick needs no per-position validation because the advance math guarantees each team exactly `total_rounds` picks and rounds = draftable spots (D91) — recorded so 068 doesn't "fix" manual picks by mistake. (8) The §7.3.8 defaults mirrored in SQL COALESCEs (`snake`/`random`/90/false) restate the spec's printed D column as defense against a bare `'{}'` blob — the settings catalog remains the source of truth on every sanctioned write path. (9) **Held-lock proof form (§4.6):** the stack vitest asserts `min(pick RTTs) < 50ms` — the client RTT upper-bounds the in-RPC lock window, a systematically held lock inflates EVERY sample, and a one-off load spike cannot flake the suite; timing via `process.hrtime.bigint()` (monotonic, outside the D3/D17 wall-clock ban — harness measurement, not league logic).

Grace contract per spec: fresh/autodraft/no-user seats autopick AT deadline (§8.5.4); a stale seat's pick holds open to deadline + grace with manual pick allowed ("reconnect restores manual control", E3). §8.7's commissioner-outage auto-pause: no fresh commish/co-commish heartbeat past grace → tick pauses the real draft + system post.

D14 reading: the writer stamps `source: provider.name` — byte-identical `'sleeper'` on the production path (`SleeperStatsProvider.name` is pinned by test); non-sleeper provider names reaching a real DB (fixture/synthetic ingestion) is a downstream-milestone concern, noted here so it's not discovered by surprise.

Field addition is within the tasks-doc §4 latitude ("Builder finalizes exact fields; names below are contractual"); consumers with a real `kickoffAt` should prefer it. Window semantics preserved exactly (day-start UTC + 36h, unchanged).

**2026-07-19 — Builder (L.A0.3):**
- **D26** Synthetic-tier design points. (1) **PRNG streams are keyed by (seed, playerId) only — never scenario id** (FNV-1a + mulberry32, `synthetic/prng.ts`): same-seed scenarios share stat lines, which is what makes the `provider_outage` back-fill proof an *equality against a same-seed `happy_path` world* instead of a self-referential recomputation. (2) **Skeleton/values split:** the timing/event skeleton (slate, kickoffs, event instants, player pool, inactives) is a fixed function of scenario id; only stat values draw from the seed — asserted by the "different seed → different lines, same skeleton" transcript test. (3) Stat lines are `floor(finalLine × progress)` — in-game deltas monotone by construction, outage recovery is cumulative back-fill for free (§23.2). (4) The synthetic tier supplies **real `kickoffAt` timestamps** (plus `gameDate` derived from them) — it's the tier that *can* (§23.6); flex/postponement reveal their new kickoff only from `announceAt`. (5) `Math.random` banned under `src/lib/leagues/**` via lint selectors. *(R22 erratum, corrected 2026-07-19: as originally shipped this was only two selectors — call + computed-property forms — strictly narrower than the D3 guard this entry called it, leaving globalThis routing, Math aliasing, and `crypto.getRandomValues` lint-clean. Widened to full R15 parity on `fix/review-findings-batch2-2026-07-19` (`bf45948`): globalThis.Math, Math alias/destructure (declarator + assignment), and crypto.getRandomValues (direct/computed/globalThis-routed) all banned; seven deliberate-violation probes shown failing. "D3-style" is accurate as of that commit.)* (6) Charted placeholder values are **absent until `chartedPostAt`, never 0** (§23.5) — a deliberate-break probe (emit 0 pre-post) was shown failing 2 tests, then reverted. (7) Base week is 2026 wk2, matching the seeded `nfl_weeks` calendar (039), so L.A0.6's calendar assertion (gate item 2c) composes without remapping. Library versioning: `SCENARIO_LIBRARY_VERSION` + per-scenario `version`, both 1. *(Both bumped to 2 on 2026-07-19: R25 changed `gameDate` from the UTC day to the ET calendar day — the first exercise of the bump-on-behavior-change contract, now enforced mechanically by the R23 golden pins.)*

**2026-07-19 — Builder (L.A0.4):**
- **D27** Fixture identity header. D6's format ("one line per provider call") gains a line-1 metadata header `{format: 'fieldscout-fixture', version: 1, provider, season, week}` — additive, versioned, and parse-rejected loudly on mismatch. Rationale: the D24 self-review note — fixtures carry source semantics downstream branching doesn't know; the recorded provider's identity must live IN the artifact, not be guessed from its path. `RecordingStatsProvider` stays a transparent wrapper (exposes the inner provider's `name`/`capabilities`; the header is written by the harness/CLI that owns the file).
- **D28** Replay identity: `FixtureReplayProvider.name = 'fixture:<recorded provider>'` (the §4 sketch's `fixture:<id>` form) — honest about data origin, so a replay ingesting into any real table writes a `source` value that says what it is. The recorded identity is exposed as `recordedProviderName` for the consumer that deliberately wants byte-identical live-session rows (L.A0.6's call when it composes the gate); the replayer never impersonates its source silently. Replay `capabilities` defaults to core_box, overridable at construction (a fixture is its source's tier; gating starts M1/D5). Also: Date revival on replay is a strict-shape reviver (full `…T…Z` ISO timestamps only — `gameDate`'s day-only strings never revive), and both before-first-recorded-response and never-recorded calls throw descriptively rather than inventing data (§23.2 "never wrong numbers"). Single-poll fixtures: the five method stamps span the poll's real wall duration and the D6 anchor maps to the FIRST stamp — replay harnesses step past the poll before reading (pinned in the cross-check test).
- **D23** Explicit grants strategy (resolves D18): migration `037_explicit_api_grants.sql` versions production's grant model — one-time catch-up `GRANT ALL` (tables/sequences/routines in `public`) for the 001–036 surface, plus `ALTER DEFAULT PRIVILEGES FOR ROLE postgres IN SCHEMA public` matching prod's `pg_default_acl` rows exactly (tables `arwdDxtm` / sequences `rwU` / functions `X` to anon/authenticated/service_role). Prod re-verified before building, per the task's stop condition: all 40 public tables (the full 001–036 surface — 36 is the count of applied migrations, not of tables; corrected per R2) grant all 8 table privileges (`arwdDxtm`, incl. PG17's MAINTAIN; "7" was a miscount, per R2) to the three API roles, and the default ACLs are exactly as D18 recorded. **ALTER DEFAULT PRIVILEGES chosen over per-table grants** because production *is* a default-ACL model: with per-table grants only, any future migration that omitted a grant (by oversight or on purpose) would silently diverge from prod, which auto-grants regardless; the versioned default ACLs replay to prod's exact model in every environment — fresh local reset, and a future re-provisioned hosted project on the new no-auto-grant default. **[Corrected 2026-07-19 per R1 — the original entry claimed the migration was "pushed to prod itself"; it was not.]** What actually happened: 037 was applied locally only; the local grant/default-ACL state after a fresh reset was verified byte-identical to prod's (grants + `pg_default_acl` comparison). **037 has NOT been pushed to prod** — prod's migration history still ends at 036. It lands with the next normal migration push, where it is an idempotent no-op (prod already holds every grant and default ACL it writes). **Implication for every future leagues migration: no per-object GRANT statements — grants stay uniformly broad and RLS is the only effective gate (spec §12; §8.2's per-table pgTAP proves it). Deliberate narrowing (e.g. an internal service-role-only function) must be an explicit REVOKE in that migration.** *Carve-out (R5, 2026-07-19): "RLS is the only effective gate" holds for tables only — SECURITY DEFINER routines bypass RLS, so every one needs in-body auth (plan §8.3, spec §12.0) plus explicit REVOKE discipline; first instance `038_notify_list_followers_auth.sql`.* pgTAP `001_api_grants.sql` pins the surface (witness-table grants, default-ACL canary table + function, RLS-still-gates behavioral check) so a CLI behavior change fails loudly there. *Process note (R6, 2026-07-19): plan §8.1's staging-clone rehearsal line was skipped for 037 without a recorded waiver — recorded now: no staging clone exists (environments are local + prod only); the fresh local `db reset` replay plus the byte-identical grants/`pg_default_acl` comparison against prod served as the rehearsal evidence. Future schema-bearing migrations (039 `nfl_weeks` onward) must rehearse on a staging clone or record this waiver explicitly per migration.* Side effects: `nfl_weeks` renumbers 037 → 038 (tasks doc §5/§6 updated); typegen deliberately not re-run — grants change no schema shape, and regenerating would clobber the hand-written alias block in `src/types/database.ts`.

**2026-07-19 — Builder (L.A0.6):**
- **D29** R30's byte-identical source-semantics opt-in has its deliberate mechanism: `impersonateRecordedProvider(replay)`, exported beside `FixtureReplayProvider` — a loudly-named delegating wrapper whose `name` is the replayer's `recordedProviderName`. Impossible to reach silently, and asserted so in the gate: `FixtureReplayProvider` has no name option (every construction path yields the `fixture:`-prefixed name — pinned), the opt-in is a separate call at the composition site (never a flag that can default on), and a runtime `instanceof` guard refuses to relabel anything that isn't a real replayer (so it can't spoof arbitrary sources). Gate pins both modes on the real 2025-wk2 fixture: honest default writes `source: 'fixture:sleeper'`; the opt-in writes `source: 'sleeper'` with rows otherwise deep-equal (the D24 byte-identical guarantee).
- **D30** Gate composition mechanics (task L.A0.6; `src/lib/leagues/gate/`, run as `npm run test:gate`): (1) The multi-poll determinism fixture (`fixtures/nfl/2026/wk02/synthetic.jsonl.gz`, 220 entries) is **fully derived** — the pure `recordHappyPathSession()` (canonical 44-poll schedule: event-instant polls + 20-virtual-minute cadence across the Sunday slate + finalize/charted/correction-close beats, all inside the seeded wk2 bounds) is re-run in-process by the gate and compared **byte-for-byte** to the checked-in file, plus SHA-256 golden pins of both the serialized fixture and the full replayed upsert-batch sequence — a tampered fixture or an unversioned scenario-library change fails mechanically (extends the R23 contract to the fixture layer); regeneration after a deliberate version bump = `npm run record:synthetic-fixture`, updating the pins in the same commit. (2) Gate item 2c consumes the 039 seed by **parsing the checked-in migration SQL** — the (2026, 2) row regex-pinned with loud failure — zero external calls, asserting against the artifact that seeds every environment; scenario `correctionWindowEndsAt` equals the seeded instant exactly, all event instants + all 220 capture stamps inside `[starts_at, correction_window_ends_at]`, and the post-window correction dated beyond it. (3) Seam replays use **identity time-anchoring** (virtual clock constructed at the recording's first stamp) so `syncLiveStats`' calendar logic behaves exactly as it did live; the D11 wall-paced 1×/4×/64× slice runs against a D17 stub wall. (4) The 2025 real-week cross-check replays as a **finalize pass** (virtual now is months past the recorded `gameDate` window, so the gate seeds a lingering live week into the stateful capturing client) — settled finals written `is_live=false`, deterministic ×3. (5) R26 writer-side back-fill: run A lives through the `provider_outage` window on a 10-min cadence (4 failed polls write nothing; `DegradationTracker` flips at exactly the 3rd, clears on recovery), run B polls the same instants minus the outage window; A's first post-recovery upsert batch equals B's batch at the same instant and strictly advances past A's last pre-outage write — cumulative catch-up proven against the capturing client, not the provider.

**2026-07-21 — Builder (L.A1.8):**

(1) **F20 discharged with the required semantics — cold buckets are DELIVERED-ZERO, no ruled alternative:** `deriveTierIndicators` emits the FULL def_pa/def_ya family as explicit 0/1 iff the family's raw source key (`def_points_allowed`/`def_yards_allowed`) is delivered; families deliver independently (PA without YA leaves only the YA rules keys pending); a derived family is fully authoritative — inbound def_pa_*/def_ya_* keys are stripped or recomputed, so an indicator can never appear without its source having been delivered this call. The pending path (§23.5/E61) stays reachable only for a genuinely unreported source. Badge semantics carry named tests that encode the specific wrong implementation (R56's falsifiability demand): an only-hot-bucket derive fails "normal-week derivation delivers the FULL family, cold buckets present-and-zero" plus the badge/pending-reachability tests — proven live as break probe 2 (41 tests fail), reverted. (3) **`perKey` is full-precision and delivered-keys-only** — `total` is the rounding of the full-precision sum, NOT Σ of rounded perKey entries (the spec's per-player rounding applies to the stored total); pending keys get no perKey entry at all, so a renderer cannot mistake pending for zero (E61). Defensive: non-finite stat/source values resolve as pending/unreported rather than poisoning a total. (4) **Bucket boundaries are explicit literals** in derive-stats.ts (reviewable), cross-checked BOTH directions against the registry by test: table keys ≡ the `storage:'derived'` key set, sizes pinned (11 PA = 7 shared + 4 ESPN, 9 YA), source keys pinned `storage:'column'`. `def_pa_0` = exactly 0; `def_ya_0_99` reads "<100" with an open lower bound (a negative-total-yards game belongs to it — tested at −12). **2026-07-21 — Builder (M1 batch-5 findings remediation, R57–R61):**
- **D58** Scoring-module failure semantics for corrupt inputs (the two R58/R59 rulings, both pinned by test). **(1) R58 — an unmappable tier source value is UNREPORTED (family absent → pending), never an all-zero delivered family.** The decision hinged on whether the published bucket tables are real-contiguous (which would justify clamping/flooring a fractional value into a bucket) or integer-gapped: they are **integer-gapped** — ESPN's Scoring-Formats article prints PA `1–6 / 7–13 / 14–17 / …` and YA `100–199 / 200–299 / …` (re-verified value-for-value by the batch-4 review), leaving real-valued gaps between adjacent buckets (13.5, 99.5), so the tables' domain is the integers and there is no defensible bucket to clamp into. Consequence: `familyMaps` requires an INTEGER inside ≥1 bucket; a fractional value is out-of-domain even when it happens to sit inside an interval (14.5 pends the family, same as 13.5 — no partial acceptance of corrupt data), and a negative PA (below `def_pa_0`'s pinned floor) pends. Pinned at the exact review-probed gap values (PA=13.5, YA=99.5, negative PA, plus in-interval-fractional PA=14.5); the one-hot property extended beyond integers (fractional/negative/non-finite sweep) asserting *delivered ⇔ integer-in-a-bucket, and delivered ⇒ exactly one hot per platform set*; a named counter-pin proves the all-zero-family state (and partial-family emission) is unconstructible. **(2) R59 — a corrupt rules coefficient THROWS (TypeError naming every offending key), the loud option:** a non-finite-number coefficient in `scoring_rules_snapshot` is corrupt config, not missing data — marking it pending would misbadge corrupt config as "waiting on stats" (the reviewer's own analysis), and letting it through NaN-poisons a total `NUMERIC(8,2)` will store (Postgres numeric has NaN — live-verified in the review). Validation runs up-front over all rules keys (including keys with no delivered stat — corrupt config is never quietly parked as pending); string-numerics (`"0.5"`) throw too, since silent coercion masks the corruption the L.A1.11 freeze should have caught. Pinned: NaN can never reach the return value — every adversarial coefficient shape either throws TypeError or yields a finite total, plus a finite-total assertion inside the 250-run property. The M4 worker (L.D1) decides catch-and-quarantine vs fail-the-run at the call site (F23's composition row is where that lands); the module's contract is simply *never emit a poisoned or silently-wrong breakdown*. **(3) R61 taken:** the round(full sum)≠Σ(rounded perKey) rule now has a stored-literal discriminating pin (`{a:0.111,b:0.111}×{a:3,b:3} → 0.67`; Σ-of-rounded yields 0.66) beside the statistical property. **(4) R57's spec fold-back is v2.8.4** (§7.3.3 parenthetical + changelog; header Version field corrected 2.8.1 → 2.8.4 in passing — it had been stale since v2.8.2).

(1) **Wire the REAL surface, don't rebuild it.** Every write goes through the existing L.A1.14/L.A1.15 routes via React Query mutation hooks: `create_league_invite`/`revoke_league_invite`/`rotate_invite_code`/`set_league_invite_slug` (`use-league-invites`), `add_placeholder_seat`/`set_member_role` (incl. the atomic commissioner transfer)/`assign_manager`/`remove_manager(mode)`/`leave_league` (`use-league-members`). The removal route's self-vs-other dispatch (D74(8)) surfaces as `useRemoveManager` (body) vs `useLeaveLeague` (no body) on the same DELETE. (2) **Reads: a direct RLS-commish-only `league_invites` SELECT** (`useLeagueInvites`, enabled only when `canManage`) — the canonical CLAUDE.md hook pattern (the `useLists` example; L.A2.3's `use-scoring-templates` reads `scoring_systems` identically). No GET-invites route was added: the §12.23 "Invites viewable by commish" policy IS the read surface and the gate (a non-commissioner gets zero rows — no existence leak), and `invited_email` is returned only here, to the commissioner. (3) **THE PRIVACY INVARIANT (§7.2/§12.23) lives in the pure ops layer, not the component.** `deriveSeats` classifies each seat claimed > invited > placeholder > open and attaches the invite (hence its email) ONLY to `invited` seats — a `claimed` seat gets `invite: null`, `emailTarget: null`, and renders `Team — display name (@username)` (the §16.4 identity rule, `formatManagerIdentity`). So even a leftover/still-pending seat-targeted invite for an already-claimed franchise — which the commissioner CAN read over RLS — never renders its email. Pinned in `invite-panel-ops.test.ts` (17 tests): the load-bearing pin is a claimed seat with a leftover email invite for its franchise → `JSON.stringify(seat)` contains no email; a still-pending one too (claimed > invited); the positive control is an invited seat that DOES surface the email (so the guard isn't vacuous). **Break probe shown:** leaking the invite onto claimed seats (`const invited = pendingInvite ?? null`) fails exactly the still-pending privacy pin, reverted. (4) **Pure-ops/UI split (roster/scoring/wizard-ops precedent) + time discipline.** All derivation is pure and colocated under `src/components/leagues/` (not `src/lib/leagues/**`, so the TimeProvider ESLint guard's scope is honored) — even so `inviteState`/`deriveSeats` take `nowMs` as a parameter (never a wall-clock read inside), so expiry classification is deterministic and pinnable; the component passes a mount-stable `useState(() => Date.now())`. (5) **Seat model:** the seat list iterates `league_members` (each materialised seat) + pads `team_count − materialised` `open` ghosts; `n/N` header = claimed managers / team_count. Placeholder/open seats carry the email-first invite form (segmented Email | Username | Assign + a copyable seat-link that mints an unrestricted seat invite and copies its `/join/<token>`); invited seats carry status + copy-link + revoke; claimed seats carry the roles UI + the D42 Remove. (6) **Takeover successor + Assign resolve `@username → user_id` via a public `profiles` SELECT** (§7.2 usernames are public; `profiles` is world-readable, 001:580) since M1 has no cross-league user picker and `remove_manager(takeover)`/`assign_manager` take a `user_id`; the RPCs stay the authority (a non-member successor / already-seated refusal surfaces as the friendly RPC message). Vacate is the default/recommended remove path (→ placeholder → invite a replacement, the D74(6) journey); retire renders DISABLED with the "after the draft" note (D42). (7) **Access (§17):** the whole panel is commissioner/co-commissioner-facing (`canManage`); the share-link card + seat affordances render only for them; the sitting commissioner's own Leave is disabled with the "transfer the commissioner role first" note (§7.2.1:192). (8) **Structured errors:** `client-fetch.ts` (`LeagueActionError` + `sendLeagueAction`) mirrors `LeaguePatchError`/`parseJsonOrThrow` (no fork) — surfaces the RPC's UX message and routes per-field 400s (the slug editor shows `fieldErrors.invite_slug`). F29/F30 (L.A2.6) and R83 (`leagues-service.ts`, never opened — the panel uses invites-/members-service) confirmed still routed. - **D81** R114 remediation (M1 batch 20, same branch `feat/M1-L.A2.6-join-page`, PR #66 — fix-then-merge). The join-by-code dialog pushed a pasted `/join/…` share link verbatim, so `encodeURIComponent('https://fieldscout.gg/join/<code>')` became the single unresolvable `[token]` segment `/join/https%3A%2F%2F…` → `get_join_preview` `{found:false}` → "This link isn't valid" on a VALID invite. **Extraction lifted into a PURE helper `extractJoinCode(raw)` in `invite-panel-ops.ts`** — placed in the §7.2 share-link section directly beside `buildJoinLink` as its exact inverse (NOT in the component's onClick, so it is pinnable). Algorithm: trim; if empty → `''`; cut everything from the first `?`/`#`; find a case-insensitive `/join/` marker; if none, return the trimmed value as-is (a bare code/token/slug); else return the last non-empty path segment after the marker (tolerates trailing slash / extra path). It preserves the segment's original case (seat tokens are case-sensitive; the RPC lower()s codes/slugs itself) and does NOT URL-encode — `JoinLeagueDialog.handleSubmit` still `encodeURIComponent`s the extracted value for the route param, and the Continue-button `disabled` gate now keys on the extracted value (a `/join/` link with no code, e.g. `https://fieldscout.gg/join/`, yields `''` → the submit no-ops instead of pushing a dead segment). **Pinned** in `invite-panel-ops.test.ts` §8 (a 16-row `it.each` table + a case-preservation pin + an empty-code pin), covering every input form the field must tolerate and **both real share artifacts** (`buildJoinLink`'s own output + the wizard's `${origin}/join/${invite_code}` shape). **Break probe:** helper forced to `return raw.trim()` (the pre-R114 behavior) fails EXACTLY the 14 link rows while the bare-code/empty rows stay green (the table discriminates), reverted. **Scope untouched:** no change to the F2 surface, the six-state claim logic, `get_join_preview`, or the v2.8.10 erratum — this is purely the join-by-code entry's input normalization. **2026-07-26 — Builder (M1 batch-16 remediation, R100–R106 on PR #60, same branch `feat/M1-L.A1.15-member-management` — fix-then-merge):**
- **D76** Batch-16 remediation mechanics (**063 amended in place** again — unreleased, prod history still ends at 036 (F12); D64(5)/D69/D72/D73/D75 amend-don't-stack precedent — plus **062's banner corrected in place** (R103), pgTAP **017 155 → 163** (+8: R100 4, R102 2, §S leave 2), +6 assertions inside the existing 19 stack-vitest cases (R100 creator-target replay; plus the `:193`→`:192` description fix, not an assertion). No typegen re-run — no signature changed. **Context:** the batch-15 re-review was a multi-agent pass CUT SHORT by a spend limit; the orchestrator directly verified the one load-bearing finding (R100) against the code, the remaining candidates were nits or refuted — verdict FIX-THEN-MERGE. **(1) R100 — the creator-target replay hole R94 left open.** R94 added the idempotent `p_role='commissioner' AND target already commissioner → transferred:false` arm but placed it BELOW the creator anti-coup guard, so replaying a completed transfer whose target is the league CREATOR (`leagues.owner_id`) still hit the guard and raised 42501 — the exact D63 idempotency break R94's banner claimed to close, surviving for the creator-target case (orchestrator-verified live repro: u0 creator+commish transfers to u1, u1 transfers back to u0, u1 replays the identical PATCH → 42501). Fixed by HOISTING the replay arm ABOVE the anti-coup guard — it performs no write and has no authz consequence (the same reasoning that already placed it ahead of the headless guard). Verified the hoist does NOT weaken §7.2: a REAL role change on the creator by a co-commissioner (`p_role <> the held role`) still falls through to the anti-coup guard and 42501s — pinned by the pre-existing creator-protection asserts (demote/remove the creator → 42501, both still green). **Why R94's pin missed it:** the existing replay pin targeted u4 (a NON-creator commissioner), so it never reached the anti-coup guard; the new pin transfers to the CREATOR then replays as a co-commissioner (pgTAP §I self-contained round-trip that restores L2 state; vitest inside the transfer/leave test). **(2) Q11 RULED (Chris) and applied — notify BOTH.** `leave_league` now notifies the departing user (`league_member`/"You left <league>"/`event:'left'`) in addition to the sitting commissioner; §S pins BOTH rows. **Fold-back choice, stated per the brief:** §7.2.1:190's "removed / left / franchise retired" categories already covered the departing user (reading (a), now authoritative) — so that half was spec-REQUIRED — but §7.2.1 named NO commissioner recipient anywhere, so the commissioner-too behaviour was spec-SILENT; I FOLDED a v2.8.9 erratum (§7.2.1:192 now names both recipients + changelog) rather than skip it, because a notification recipient set is a product contract. **F37 discharged.** **(3) R101 — the §R structural pins were unbounded** (`strpos(substr(prosrc, strpos(prosrc,'FOR UPDATE')), 'v_actor_role NOT IN') > 0`): no upper bound and no RAISE assertion, so a mutant sinking the re-gate below the writes OR neutering the RAISE body to NULL passed. Each of the four now pins the re-gate to the window (first FOR UPDATE, first write) AND requires a '42501' inside the re-gate branch (between `v_actor_role NOT IN` and its END IF) — catching BOTH mutant classes; per-RPC first-write markers recorded in the §R header. **(4) R102 — R91/R92 pinned on BOTH axes:** each retargeted assert keeps its `throws_like` message pin AND gains a companion `throws_ok(…, 'P0001', null, …)` SQLSTATE pin — the code assertion was ADDED beside the message, not traded for it. **(5) R103–R106 (doc nits):** D75(7)'s "three 062 pointers / +71" corrected to FOUR with the per-pointer non-uniform shifts (R104); the `§7.2.1:193` citation in `members-api-db.test.ts` repointed to `:192` — the one site the batch-15 R99 sweep missed (R105, and re-verified no other `:193` survives outside R99's own historical finding text); **062's banner miscitation FIXED IN PLACE** (`061:159`, a SELECT → `061:166`, the P0002 RAISE) so the hand-off recorded only in 063's prose is discharged rather than left as a prose-only obligation (R51/R103), and 063's note updated to say it was corrected; `leave_league`'s R90-sweep comment reworded to the TRUE precondition — "ever held commissioner OR co-commissioner powers" (a co-commissioner can mint a placeholder seat and needs no transfer to leave), not "was the sitting commissioner and transferred it away" (R106). **(6) Falsifiability:** the R100 hoist reverted (anti-coup guard back above the replay arm) → the new creator-target replay pin fails in BOTH suites (pgTAP 017 raises the anti-coup 42501; vitest `creatorReplay.status` 403 ≠ 200), shown then reverted. Full green after revert.

**2026-07-26 — Builder (M1 batch-15 remediation, R90–R99 on PR #60, same branch `feat/M1-L.A1.15-member-management` — fix-then-merge):**
- **D75** Batch-15 remediation mechanics (**063 amended in place** — unreleased, prod history still ends at 036 (F12); D64(5)/D69/D72/D73 amend-don't-stack precedent — plus `members-service.ts`, pgTAP **017 142 → 155**, and 3 new assertions inside the existing 19 stack-vitest cases. No typegen re-run: no signature changed). **(1) R90 — the one real invariant hole, and the fixture blindness that hid it.** `leave_league`/`remove_manager` moved `teams.owner_id` only on the departing member's OWN franchise, so every placeholder seat they had minted as commissioner stayed owned by a proven non-member on `teams.owner_id REFERENCES profiles(id) ON DELETE CASCADE` (001:495) — that account's deletion would destroy those franchises AND, via `league_members.team_id ON DELETE SET NULL` (052:67), leave cache rows with `team_id` NULL: a seat with no franchise, invisible to every capacity count, i.e. **the exact shape the S2 policy drop / erratum v2.8.8 / D74(3) exists to make unrepresentable**. 063's own banner named the hazard as the reason it moves `owner_id` at all, then covered one franchise out of N. Fixed as ONE league-wide sweep per departure path (`WHERE league_id = … AND owner_id = <departing> AND id <> v_team.id` → the acting commissioner; `leave_league` guards on a non-NULL commissioner). **Why 142 asserts missed it:** every L2 seat in the fixture was later claimed or assigned, which moves `owner_id` as a side effect — so the residual could only appear on a seat that is minted and then NEVER filled, a shape no fixture had. Both new fixtures ('Ledger Seat', 'Creator Seat') exist purely to be un-filled. Lesson for the ledger's sake: *a fixture that always exercises the happy path of a side effect cannot detect a missing sweep.* **(2) R93 — authorization re-read UNDER the lock, all five RPCs.** Four of them evaluated `is_league_commish` BEFORE `leagues … FOR UPDATE` and never again, so an actor demoted while blocked on that lock still completed one privileged write; `leave_league` alone had it right and its comment said why. Each of the four now re-reads the actor's role after the lock and gates on it (`NOT IN ('commissioner','co_commissioner')` → 42501). Two deliberate choices: the pre-lock `is_league_commish` call is **kept**, because dropping it would make a nonexistent league answer P0002/404 instead of 42501 — an existence leak the SQLSTATE convention explicitly forbids — so it is a fast-fail, not the gate; and `set_member_role`/`remove_manager` fold their existing exact-role read INTO the re-gate rather than reading twice (that read is what the creator anti-coup guards key on, which is the aggravating half: a stale actor reading `'manager'` was strictly MORE powerful than a legitimate co-commissioner, since every anti-coup guard keys on `'co_commissioner'`). **(3) R93's pin is STRUCTURAL, and that is a considered exception.** The guard fires only when a demotion commits while a call waits on the league row; both reads hit the same table in the same transaction, so no single-session pgTAP fixture can reach it and a two-session race test would be nondeterministic (the house does not ship flaky pins). §R therefore asserts, per RPC, that the actor-role re-read appears AFTER the first `FOR UPDATE` in `pg_proc.prosrc`. It is falsifiable in exactly the claimed way — deleting OR hoisting a re-gate fails exactly one assert, which names its RPC (probe 2 shown). **(4) R94 — replaying a completed transfer.** 063's banner promised D63 idempotency for `set_member_role`, but the headless-league guard was unconditional in `p_role` and sat ahead of the idempotent arm, so the natural retry — the transferor's own client, now a co_commissioner, resending the same PATCH — died on P0001/400 with copy asserting the target is not commissioner. A `p_role = 'commissioner' AND target already commissioner` arm now returns `{transferred:false}`; it sits AFTER the creator anti-coup check (deliberately conservative) and leaves the headless guard intact for the demote it exists for (still pinned). **(5) R91/R92 — two asserts that could not fail for their stated reason.** Both were `throws_ok(…, 'P0001', null)` on fixtures that satisfied two guards at once, so deleting the guard under test left a DIFFERENT guard raising the same SQLSTATE. **Both halves were required and both were taken:** retarget the fixture (an EMPTY, stint-free seat in the other league, with an actor/target seated in neither) AND assert the message with `throws_like`. Mutants shown failing (probes 4 and 5). The rule this generalises to, for the falsifiability floor: *an SQLSTATE-only assertion is only falsifiable when the fixture can trip exactly one guard* — otherwise pin the message. **(6) R96 HALTED at its embedded ruling question — see Q11.** The takeover/vacate notification ROWS are now pinned (recipient + outcome category), because the notifier swallows every error so a broken INSERT was invisible in both suites. The LEAVE path's recipient is deliberately NOT pinned: §7.2.1:190 lists `left` among the categories the *removed user* is notified with while `leave_league` notifies only the commissioner, and pinning today's behaviour would cement a reading nobody has ruled. Both new queries are scoped so they assert nothing about the leave notification either way. **(7) R97/R98/R99 — the R84/R89 attestation class, swept rather than spot-fixed.** The three findings were re-verified and then EVERY factual claim in 063's banner, the 017 header, D74 and the new F-rows was re-checked against the shipped tree — which turned up more than the review caught: the 017 header's falsifiability map was re-derived section by section (8 of 10 bullets had pointed at the wrong letter; the rewritten map covers all 19 groups A–S and says which are fixtures-only), 063's held-lock note now resolves all eleven `FOR UPDATE` sites by table instead of asserting a two-table order that was never true, and the citation sweep repointed four §7.2/§7.2.1 spec lines (188/190/192/165), FOUR 062 pointers invalidated by THIS PR's own in-place amendment of 062 (the amendment inserted at several points below ~260, so the shift is NOT uniform — 722→801 is +79, 790→864 +74, 397→468 +71, 150-159→176-184 +26; corrected from the "three / +71" miscount per R104), and one inherited error (062's banner miscites the third P0002 precedent as 061:159, a SELECT; the RAISE is 061:166 — corrected in 063's copy, left in 062's, recorded here). **The lesson worth keeping: an in-place amendment silently invalidates every line citation into the amended file, including ones written in the same PR — line-number citations into a file the same PR edits must be re-resolved at the END of the work, not when written.**

**2026-07-26 — Builder (L.A1.15):**
- **D74** Member-management mechanics (`063_league_member_management.sql` + pgTAP **017** (142 asserts as shipped by this task; **155** after the batch-15 remediation — D75) + `members-service.ts` + 3 routes + stack vitest `members-api-db.test.ts` (19); **062 amended in place**, unreleased per F12 — the 061/060 precedent). **(1) The 062 defect this task had to fix first, and the review-coverage lesson it teaches.** `claim_league_invite`'s seat-targeted branch open-coded a blind `INSERT INTO league_members`, so a §7.2:170-shaped placeholder seat (`user_id NULL`, **`team_id` SET**) was UNCLAIMABLE: `UNIQUE(league_id, team_id)` raised 23505, claim's own `EXCEPTION WHEN unique_violation` arm swallowed it as `seat_filled`, **expired the perfectly good invite** (`expires_at = now()`) and notified the commissioner about a conflict that never happened — re-proven live this session inside `BEGIN … ROLLBACK` against merged `main` before a line was written. **This survived an 88-agent adversarial review of PR #58 because placeholder seats did not exist yet** — no fixture could reach the branch. Record it as a review-coverage lesson, not a reviewer failure: a defect that is unreachable *today* becomes reachable the moment the next task lands, so a review scoped to "what is reachable now" systematically misses this class. The counter-practice: when a task ships a branch keyed on a row shape another task will introduce, the review should construct that shape by hand. **(2) One seating implementation, not three** (CLAUDE.md's no-near-duplicate rule; C9 of the build brief): rather than a third copy, `seat_league_member_internal` gained `p_team_id DEFAULT NULL` — NULL = create a franchise (join/general claim, unchanged), non-NULL = stint-first (E54's named mechanism preserved) → **FILL** the placeholder cache row (`UPDATE … WHERE user_id IS NULL`, INSERT only if no row exists) → `owner_id` follows the manager and an `'orphaned'` franchise returns to `'active'` (§7.2.1(c) "orphaned is a holding state that resolves into (a) or (b)" — a claim IS resolution (a)). Both `claim_league_invite` and the new `assign_manager` now route through it, so faab seeding, stint ordering and `clock_timestamp()` cannot drift. pgTAP 016's helper-signature/ACL pins were moved deliberately, not discovered. **(3) 054's two placeholder carve-out policies DROPPED** — spec erratum **v2.8.8**, ruled under the EXISTING Q8 ruling rather than as a new product decision. Both required `team_id IS NULL`: a seat with no franchise, which contradicts §7.2:170, is invisible to every capacity check in the build (061's F28 floor, 062's join + claim checks, and `add_placeholder_seat` all count `teams WHERE league_id = X AND status <> 'retired'`), and is unreachable by `assign_manager`. A commissioner could therefore POST directly to `/rest/v1/league_members` and over-subscribe past `team_count`; meanwhile an RPC-created placeholder was never client-deletable by the sibling policy anyway. D52(3)'s stated rationale ("the one client-shaped act with no stint implications") is falsified once a placeholder seat IS a franchise. `league_members` is now SELECT-only on the client; pgTAP 006 (81 → 77 asserts), 008 (27 → 28, its "privileged reseat" shape reconciled to the one `remove_manager(vacate)` actually writes — R45 staleness class) and 017 pin the new surface per role. **(4) The commissioner transfer verb** — the one genuinely new contract. §7.2 names "Exactly one commissioner" but no transfer; §7.2.1:192 REQUIRES one ("a commissioner cannot leave without first transferring the commissioner role"), so without it `leave_league` is unsatisfiable and no commissioner could ever leave a league. `set_member_role(p_role)` therefore accepts `commissioner | co_commissioner | manager`, and `p_role = 'commissioner'` is an **atomic transfer** — demote the incumbent to `co_commissioner`, promote the target, in one statement pair under the league-row `FOR UPDATE`, **demote-first** so the new partial unique index never sees two. **Only the sitting commissioner may transfer** (a co-commissioner promoting themselves is a coup → 42501); the sitting commissioner's own row changes ONLY by transfer (a demote would leave the league headless); nobody may `remove_manager` the sitting commissioner. Stated here so Chris can overturn it in one line if he reads §7.2 differently. **(5) Contracts the spec does not print, Builder-finalized (D72 slug precedent):** franchise name for a new seat = caller-supplied, else deterministic `Team N` (N = non-retired franchise count + 1 — matches §7.2:165's own invite copy "You've been invited to manage Team 4"); `add_placeholder_seat` is gated to `setup`/`scheduled` (the D72 class — creating a franchise mid-draft would corrupt M2/M4 machinery); a takeover successor inherits the SEAT but not the ROLE (`role` resets to `'manager'` — powers never ride along with a franchise); `vacate` moves `teams.owner_id` to the acting commissioner (the column is NOT NULL and leaving it on the removed user would let their profile deletion CASCADE the franchise away). **(6) `takeover` = direct reseat, not invite-minting.** §15.1's DELETE body (`{ mode, successor_user_id?, reason }`) names a successor, so `takeover` REQUIRES `successor_user_id` and touches no `league_invites` row; the "invite a replacement" journey is `vacate` + the existing `create_league_invite(target_team_id)`, which is exactly what L.A2.5 composes. This is also what keeps the lock ordering safe: none of the five RPCs locks an invite row, so none can deadlock against an in-flight claim. **Lock order corrected per R98 (the original clause here and in 063's banner both misstated it):** the real order is `leagues` → `league_members` → `teams`, and only three of the five take the middle lock — `set_member_role` (leagues + the target member row, no teams lock at all), `remove_manager` and `leave_league` (all three); `add_placeholder_seat` takes leagues alone and `assign_manager` leagues + teams. Nothing can invert the pair because everything serializes on the league row first, and no RPC outside 063 locks `league_members` — so the safety conclusion survives; it was the artifact a sixth writer would consult that was wrong. 063's banner now resolves all eleven `FOR UPDATE` sites explicitly. **(7) These RPCs RAISE; they do not return outcome jsonb.** The 062 claim/join contract exists because E54's refusal must PERSIST writes — no refusal here has side effects worth keeping, and L.A2.5 renders the unavailable `retire` mode as a DISABLED control with an "after the draft" note rather than as a submitted state. One style per route family; the service maps 42501 → 403, **P0002 → 404 (before any field-error arm — R87)**, P0001/22023 → 400. **No new field-error markers were added** (`RPC_FIELD_ERRORS`/`PATCH_FIELD_ERRORS` are untouched — stated explicitly because R84 was filed for prose attesting a marker that did not exist). **(8) `leave_league` routing.** §15.1 prints no leave endpoint — the only removal verb is `DELETE /api/leagues/[id]/members/[mid]` — so the route dispatches on whose membership `[mid]` is: the caller's own → `leave_league` (`end_reason='left'`, no mode; §7.2.1 gives the leaver no say in the franchise's outcome), anyone else's → the commish-gated `remove_manager(mode)`. No `/leave` route was invented. `leave_league` takes **no** `p_user_id` at all, so it can never become a kick path, and it takes the league row FOR UPDATE *before* reading the caller's role so a concurrent transfer cannot make them commissioner between check and write. The solo commissioner (day one of every league) is pointed at deleting the league rather than stranded. **(9) `end_reason` is a permanent history contract** — §12.22 prints the four values as a bare comment with no mapping, and L.A1.15 is the first writer of the column in the repo: `replaced` = takeover, `kicked` = vacate (removed with no successor), `left` = voluntary leave, `seat_retired` = reserved for M4's `retire_franchise` (F31). Now CHECK-enforced. History Mode (M5) reads this mapping. **(10) What actually revokes access in M1** — stated identically in the 063 banner, the pgTAP 017 header and the F3 discharge because §7.2.1:188's wording invites the opposite claim: "write access derives from the open stint" is NOT what the code does. `is_league_member`/`is_league_commish` read `league_members` only, and `team_managers`' own policy is gated on `is_league_member`, so **closing a stint revokes nothing**. Access ends because the departing user's cache row loses its `user_id` — in the SAME transaction as the stint close, so history and access never diverge. pgTAP 017 §M asserts BOTH halves (stint closed alone → still sees everything; RPC → zero rows) so the doc can never quietly drift from the code (**F35** if a future milestone moves an RLS predicate onto stints). **(11) The departed creator keeps SELECT on the league** (`leagues` policy has an `owner_id = auth.uid()` branch and no RPC moves `owner_id`) — accepted deliberately, not overlooked: `leagues.owner_id` is the CREATOR record that §7.2's anti-coup rule keys on ("The original creator can never be removed by a co-commissioner"), so transferring it with the commissioner role would break that guard for exactly the case it exists to cover. Read-only, no membership, no member rows visible; rotating the invite code is the remedy for the share link. Pinned in BOTH suites and filed as **F36**. **"No write path" made precise per R90/R98:** it covers every LEAGUE surface (`leagues`, `league_members`, `team_managers` and all five RPCs key on `is_league_member`/`is_league_commish`) — it never covered `team_lineups`, whose 001:856-861 FOR ALL policy keys on `teams.owner_id` with no `league_id IS NULL` scoping (unlike the teams policy 053:71-72 D35b narrowed). Before R90 a departed member kept lineup writes on any franchise still pointing at them; the R90 league-wide owner sweep closes that, and 017 §L now pins that NO franchise in the league is still owned by them. The policy surface itself remains M4's (C12/F18). **(12) R43-class constraints taken now** (D55(2a)/D69): `league_members.role` CHECK, the `one_commissioner_per_league` partial unique index (§7.2's invariant had ZERO DB backing — the API refusal was all that stood between the data and a two-commissioner league), and `team_managers.end_reason`/`role` CHECKs. Each is pinned by a PRIVILEGED (RPC-context) write, so an API-only guard would pass the role tests and fail these. **(13) Falsifiability:** two deliberate-break probes shown failing and reverted — deleting the creator-protection branch fails exactly the co-commissioner-actor test (and nothing else), and swapping `clock_timestamp()` for `now()` in the successor-stint insert fails exactly the stint-ordering pin. Every role fixture in the role-invariant group is a seated CO-commissioner (a plain manager is refused by `is_league_commish` first, which would have made the guards vacuous); the capacity boundary asserts no-write at one-past, not merely an error; the closed stint's `started_at` is golden-pinned to a literal captured before the removal so a merge-in-place implementation fails even though the row count matches.

Route mapping: not_found→404 · mismatch→403 · expired/revoked/spent→410 · seat_filled/seat_unavailable/league_full/joins_closed→409; the body always carries reason/message. (2) **Claim order: already-member FIRST, then the F6 algebra** (revoked → expired-at-instant → spent), then identity match, then seating — a successful single-use claim leaves the invite spent, so the claimer's own retry must short-circuit to `{ok:true, already_member:true}` (D63) BEFORE the spent check; no use_count bump on replay (pinned). (3) **F6 expiry semantics DEFINED: the invite expires AT `expires_at`** (`now() >= expires_at` refuses — exactly-at is expired); pinned at 1s-before/exactly-at/1s-past via pgTAP's txn-frozen now(). Claimed-then-revoked multi-use: revocation refuses FURTHER claims even with uses remaining; prior claimers keep their seats (not retroactive) — both pinned. (4) **E54 mechanics:** the stint INSERT runs FIRST in the seat-targeted block so an occupied seat hits `one_open_stint_per_team` (the spec-named mechanism; `league_members`' UNIQUE(league_id, team_id) is the second net, same handler); the 23505 handler marks the invite expired (`expires_at = now()` — instantly expired under (3)), notifies the commissioner (type `league_invite`, event `seat_conflict`), and returns seat_filled. Both notification inserts (this + the username-invite notification) go through a guarded internal helper (`notify_league_invite_internal`, EXCEPTION WHEN OTHERS → NULL) — a notification failure can never break the parent write. (5) **F3 discharge (claim half): stint inserts use `clock_timestamp()`, not now()** — now() is txn-frozen, so a remove→re-claim inside one txn would reuse the prior stint's `started_at` and 23505 on UNIQUE(team_id, user_id, started_at); pinned by the re-claim path (two rows, one open, DISTINCT started_at). Close-before-open has no claim-side application (claims never close stints — that is L.A1.15's remove/assign, where the other half of F3 lives). (6) **Capacity under the league-row lock:** claim/join take the league FOR UPDATE before counting active (non-retired) franchises, so concurrent joins serialize and teams ≤ team_count holds; the same lock serializes against 061's F28 shrink floor (`team_count >= seated count`, both writers). At capacity → friendly "league is full", ZERO writes (D47 — no unseated member rows). (8) **D46 writer:** re-creating an identical LIVE identity-targeted invite (same league/team/email-or-username, unrevoked, unexpired, uses remaining) re-sends instead of duplicating — `last_sent_at = now()` on the existing row, `resent: true` (created_at stays the initial-send record); open/general links never dedupe (multiple live links are legitimate). (10) **All code/slug/email/username matching uses `lower()` text comparison, not citext operators:** under `SET search_path = ''` the citext `=` operator (extensions schema) is not in the operator lookup path, and a citext-vs-text comparison silently degrades to case-SENSITIVE text equality — lower() is resolution-proof and D36-literal; case-insensitivity pinned both directions (uppercase stored/lowercase JWT and vice versa). (11) **get_join_preview (the §4.1 anon carve-out):** REVOKE FROM PUBLIC + explicit GRANT to anon/authenticated; returns EXACTLY `{found, type, status, league_name, team_label, inviter_name, seats_open}` (or `{found:false}`) — the F2 pin is exact-jsonb equality AS ANON plus a named never-exposes negative (invited_email/invited_username/token/ids); status vocabulary ok|revoked|expired|spent|seat_filled|league_full|joins_closed feeds the L.A2.6 claim-page states pre-auth. Preview is not a Next route — /join/[token] calls the RPC over the anon key. (12) **`teams.owner_id` follows the current manager on seat claims** (informational on league teams — D35b left them with no client write path); `claimed_by`/`claimed_at` = the MOST RECENT claim (§12.23 prints single columns; use_count is the aggregate record). (13) **Email seam (D37/Q5):** `src/lib/email/email-sender.ts` — `EmailSender` interface + `DevLogEmailSender` + pure `renderLeagueInviteEmail` (league name, team label, `/join/[token]` claim link) + `buildClaimUrl` (NEXT_PUBLIC_APP_URL); the route-side send runs AFTER the RPC commits (send-intent already on the row per D46) and a sender failure surfaces as `email_sent: false`, never a failed request; vendor adapter stays F15. (14) **pgTAP mechanics learned:** `results_eq` over `pg_proc.proname::text` hits a name-vs-text collation conflict inside its record comparison (COLLATE markers don't survive into row IS DISTINCT FROM) — the nine-function search_path pin became a count+bool_and exact-value ok(); RLS-scoped inspection reads (league_invites commish-only, team_managers/league_members member-only, notifications owner-only) need `reset role` wraps or they NULL-fail; temp tables created under the authenticated role need explicit GRANTs before an anon section reads them, and league codes must be captured privileged (leagues SELECT is member-scoped). Pinned in pgTAP and again at the API layer in `scheduled`. (5) **The lifecycle path is the named enforcement point** (task item 3; 059's banner): `status: 'scheduled'` runs `validateLeagueSettings` over the CURRENT STORED settings BEFORE `set_league_status` — proven with a privileged-corruption fixture (service-role seam break) the route validator refuses per-field with the RPC never reached; a missing draft instant surfaces 059's P0001 as a per-field 400 on `draft.draft_scheduled_at`; setup ↔ scheduled pinned BOTH ways; drafting-state settings/status PATCHes → 409s on exact message markers. (6) **Gate item 3 discharged:** the round-trip suite imports the EXPORTED enumeration fixture (D60(10) — re-enumerating locally is the named anti-pattern) and sweeps EVERY §7.3 field as its own test (the fixture's every-leaf-non-default design rule means a field dropped anywhere in split → RPC → DB → merge falls back to its default and fails exactly its own test — the DoD probe proved this live), plus: template-change round-trip + the rejected personal-id case, `max_teams = team_count` by DIRECT DB read after the team_count PATCH, ranged-field boundaries as two cross-field-consistent min/max composites (sanity-pinned valid first) + the exact week-18 playoff-arithmetic edge, one rejected out-of-range case per §7.3 field group (each with a no-write pin), and the L.A1.6 hand-off — a freshly-defaulted leagues row's `roster_settings` (040's column DEFAULT) deep-equals `DEFAULT_ROSTER_SETTINGS`. (7) **Held-lock (plan §8.3):** FOR UPDATE held across exactly the one UPDATE + the conditional re-seed (the D68(5)/soft_delete shape) — cited in the 061 banner. §4.2 has no new target (no new table; policies unchanged). Typegen: the inherited regen re-proven byte-identical to a fresh `--local` regen over 001–061, alias block intact (R68 form). (8) **Session mechanics:** this task FINISHED an inherited half-session — a prior Builder died three times on transient API errors after writing 061/015/service/route/typegen (uncommitted); every inherited artifact was re-verified against the actual tree before the round-trip/lifecycle suites, proofs, probe, and docs were done fresh. No §6 ledger rows name L.A1.13 (audited — none; F25 was already ✅). *(Annotation 2026-07-25, batch-13 review R79 → D71: the (1) "FULL L.A1.6 contract object" wire contract was UNENFORCED — every schema field default-fills, so a partial body silently reset the whole surface to catalog defaults; superseded by D71's merge-over-current partial semantics. The (4) re-seed rationale folded into the spec as v2.8.7 per R80.)*

**2026-07-25 — Builder (M1 batch-13 remediation, R79–R82 on PR #56, same branch `feat/M1-L.A1.13-settings-patch` — fix-then-merge):**
- **D71** PATCH settings partial semantics — **merge-over-current** (the R79 ruling; supersedes D70(1)'s full-object wire contract on the partial-body point). (1) **The hole:** the declared "settings = the FULL L.A1.6 contract object" contract was unenforced — every `leagueSettingsSchema` field default-fills, so a one-key body (`{settings:{median_game:true}}`) parsed clean and silently reset the ENTIRE settings surface to catalog defaults (review live-proof: team_count 14→12, faab_budget 777→100 with the §12.2 re-seed amplifying it to every seat's balance, and `draft.draft_scheduled_at` NULLED while status stayed `scheduled` — exactly the state the gate-item-3 enforcement point exists to prevent). (2) **The choice, merge over reject-partial:** merge is the REST-correct PATCH reading, matches what L.A2.4's settings panel will send (per-group saves — the panel can send just its group), and a FULL object merges to itself so the sanctioned GET-detail → edit → PATCH-back round-trip is byte-identical to before; reject-partial would have forced every client to always send full objects and still needed a deep-required schema variant to close the NESTED variant of the same hole (a partial `draft` block default-filling `draft_scheduled_at` to null). (3) **Mechanics:** the settings path ALWAYS reads the CURRENT stored settings first (RLS-scoped client, same service call) and deep-merges the raw partial over them — plain objects merge recursively; arrays, scalars, and null REPLACE wholesale (null is a VALUE for the nullable fields — deliberately NOT RFC-7386 key-deletion); `undefined` skipped (unrepresentable in JSON). The MERGED WHOLE then re-enters the ONE canonical strict schema (unknown keys at ANY level reject; no default can ever fill — the current row supplies every key) and `validateLeagueSettings` (cross-field rules run against the merged whole). The partial input is validated POST-merge through the single catalog schema — no hand-maintained deep-partial schema, so the D60(10) anti-pattern stays dead; the wire schema's `settings` is now `z.looseObject({})` (any JSON object; shape enforced post-merge). (4) **Deliberate consequences:** (a) read-first means an RLS-invisible league uniformly **404s** on the settings path (was 403 via the RPC's 42501) — matching the status path and GET detail and fixing the pre-D71 inconsistency where a scoring-only PATCH already 404'd on invisible leagues while a settings PATCH 403'd; no existence leak either way (non-member and nonexistent stay indistinguishable), and a MEMBER non-commish still 403s from the RPC — pinned with a privileged-seat fixture (service-role `league_members` insert; real-user membership is RPC-only until L.A1.14). (b) The read-merge-write is NOT held under the RPC's row lock — D70's last-write-wins-wholesale concurrency caveat extends to interleaved read-merge-write (single-commissioner M1 reality; revisit if co-commissioner concurrent edits become real). (5) **Pins (round-trip suite 68 → 74):** the EXACT R79 probe is the regression trap (a scheduled 14-team faab-777 league with a draft instant: `{settings:{median_game:true}}` → 200 changing ONLY `median_game` — whole-object equality with one delta, plus by-name pins on team_count/max_teams 14, budget 777, seat balance 777 NOT re-seeded, draft instant + `scheduled` status intact), nested one-key merge inside `draft` (instant survives), merged-whole cross-field reject (`{regular_season_weeks:15}` alone breaks the Q10 seam post-merge → per-field 400 + no-write), unknown-key rejects top-level AND nested, and full-object identity. Deliberate-break probe: reverting the merge to the pre-D71 parse fails 4/5 trap tests on exactly the team_count 14→12 reset (shown, reverted). (6) **Companions:** R80 → spec erratum **v2.8.7** (§12.2 re-seed folded in, one sentence + changelog — D70(4) is the recorded rationale); R81 → ledger **F28** (shrink-below-occupancy); R82 → the top-level unknown-key PATCH pin in the wire-shape test (the R77-class discriminating pin: sent alongside a valid key, a plain z.object would strip-and-200). No migration/pgTAP/typegen change — 061, 015, and the generated Functions surface are untouched.
- **D55** Migration 056 mechanics (`056_league_weeks.sql` + pgTAP 010). (1) `league_weeks` RLS ships with **ONE** policy — member SELECT (`is_league_member`, not `is_league_commish` — unlike `league_invites`, §12.17 names no seat-targeted-privacy reason to narrow the read) — and no write policy for any role, including the commissioner: the task text says the table is "populated by M4's schedule/`league-week-advance` machinery," naming no client-write shape, so this follows `team_managers`'/`league_invites`' fully-RPC(-eventually-cron)-only precedent. (2) Two additive deviations from §12.17's printed DDL, both cheap and pinned, neither new product surface: (a) a CHECK enumerating `status`'s four values — §12.17 prints them only as a SQL comment, the exact R43 gap (`teams.status` silently accepted `'banana'` until 054 fixed it there); fixed here at creation instead of waiting for a review pass. Legal *transitions* between those four values are deliberately NOT enforced — no writer of any kind exists yet (RLS denies every client write; no RPC in this task calls one), so a transition trigger would have no legitimate caller to validate against until M4's `league-week-advance` job lands (D42/D53 scope-cut precedent). (b) `FOREIGN KEY (season, week) REFERENCES nfl_weeks(season, week)` — §12.17 prints season/week as unconstrained integers despite `nfl_weeks` (039/§12.20) being the spec's own calendar backbone (§23.3: "no code ever infers 'current week' from wall-clock math"); the composite FK is free (`nfl_weeks(season, week)` is already its PK, no new index needed) and makes a league week naming a nonexistent NFL week impossible. (3) pgTAP file is **010**, not the "009" tasks-M1's task text names — 009 was already taken by `league_invites` (L.A1.4, landed same session); same class of numbering drift already flagged elsewhere in that doc for migrations, now hit on the pgTAP sequence too. (4) Typegen: genuinely new table shape, full regen ran; `LeagueWeek` alias inserted alphabetically between `LeagueMember` and `List`; diff confirmed additive-only.

| F50 | **`draft_tick` ARM 1.5's claim shape cannot rotate a >25-candidate outage batch** — the outage arm claims with `LIMIT 25` and NO ORDER BY, loop, or `v_seen` (deliberate M2 shape: an outage pause is idempotent and not deadline-urgent, and the ≤25-draft M2 gate means the candidate set can never exceed the batch). BEYOND the gate the recorded "overflow candidates are claimed on the next 5s tick" rationale is unguaranteed: un-ordered `LIMIT 25` claims have no rotation, so ≥25 candidates that never leave the candidate set could recur in every batch while REAL outage candidates starve — and a starved candidate whose deadline is due is autopicked by ARM 2 **in the same tick**, the exact "nothing runs unsupervised" failure §8.7 exists to prevent. The one KNOWN permanent-candidate class (a live non-mock draft under a soft-deleted league — reachable today because `soft_delete_league` (060:323-359) has **no draft-status gate**) was closed at the claim itself in the batch-5 addendum (R141: the `leagues.deleted_at` EXISTS mirrored from the body; pinned in 023 §H on the LD fixture); what remains is any candidate the body persistently fails on (recurring `outage_failures` rows never leave the set). Fix shape at discharge: give the claim the ARM-2 loop + `v_seen` rotation (or an ORDER BY on staleness). **Folded-in 060 observation (noted, deliberately NOT changed in M2):** `soft_delete_league` deleting a league mid-live-draft leaves a ZOMBIE live draft that no tick arm will ever advance or pause (ARM 2 body-skips deleted leagues; ARM 1.5 now never claims them) and no surface lists — whether league delete should refuse, auto-pause, or reset a live/paused draft is a product call the discharging session (or any earlier task touching 060) should raise as a spec question rather than improvise | R141 (M2 batch-5 addendum) | M7 / L.F1 §22.6 load gate — the first milestone whose scale can exceed the ≤25-draft M2 gate (the M7 canary cohort is 25 leagues and GA follows; M5 transactions has no load-work identity) | ⬜ Open |
| F37 | **The VOLUNTARY-LEAVE notification's recipient is UNRULED and therefore UNPINNED** — §7.2.1:190 lists `left` among the categories the *removed user* is notified with, while `leave_league` (063) notifies only the sitting commissioner and writes nothing to the departing user. Filed as spec question **Q11** (options + recommendation (a): notify the leaver too, keeping the commissioner notification). pgTAP 017 §S pins the takeover and vacate notification rows — whose recipient is the removed user under BOTH readings — and deliberately scopes its queries so neither asserts anything about the leave path. **The session that applies the ruling must add the §S pin at the same time**, or the guarded notifier (which swallows every error) leaves the leave notification unpinned indefinitely. Gates one pin, not a lane: no task waits on it | R96 → Q11 (batch 15, D75(6)) | Q11 ruling (M1 batch 16) | ✅ Done (2026-07-26, M1 batch-16 remediation, PR #60 — **Q11 RULED by Chris: notify BOTH the departing user and the sitting commissioner**. `leave_league` (063) now calls the notifier for the departing user too (`league_member` / "You left <league>" / `event:'left'`), keeping the pre-existing commissioner notification. pgTAP 017 §S now pins BOTH the departing-user row AND the commissioner row on §K's L1 leave (privileged 016-style read, `event='left'`-scoped so it isolates from u5's earlier `removed` row); the guarded notifier's blanket EXCEPTION no longer hides the leave INSERT. Spec erratum **v2.8.9** (§7.2.1:192 names both recipients + changelog). D76) |

### Blocker
